\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\log\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{n}f_{j}(Y_{i}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i})^{\ind\{R_{i}=1\}}}{\prod_{i=1}^{n}f_{0}(Y_{i}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i})^{\ind\{R_{i}=1\}}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\log\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\frac{f_{j}(Y_{i}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i})}{f_{0}(Y_{i}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i})}\right)^{\ind\{R_{i}=1\}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\ind\{R_{i}=1\}\cdot\log\frac{f_{j}(Y_{i}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i})}{f_{0}(Y_{i}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i})}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\ind\{R_{i}=1\}\cdot\log\frac{f_{j}(Y_{i}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i})}{f_{0}(Y_{i}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i})}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i},\bar{H}_{i-1}\right]\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\log\frac{f_{j}(Y_{i}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i})}{f_{0}(Y_{i}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i})}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i}\right]\cdot\mathbb{P}\left(R_{i}=1\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i},\bar{H}_{i-1}\right)\right],

(A.37)

where in the last equation we used the fact that
R_{i}\perp Y_{i}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i},\bar{H}_{i-1}
and
(X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i},Y_{i})\perp\bar{H}_{i-1}
, and the expectation is with respect to
f^{j}
.
Note that
\mathbb{E}\left[\log\frac{f_{j}(Y_{i}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i})}{f_{0}(Y_{i}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i})}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i}\right]
is the K-L divergence between two Bernoulli distributions
f_{j}(Y_{i}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i})
and
f_{0}(Y_{i}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i})
. Since
\delta_{j}:=c\left(\frac{s\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{1/2}\frac{\bm{\omega}_{j}^{T}\bm{z}}{\sqrt{s}}=o(1)
uniformly over
\bm{z}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\log\frac{f_{j}(Y_{i}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i})}{f_{0}(Y_{i}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i})}\mid X_{i}=x,\bm{Z}_{i}=\bm{z}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f_{j}(y=1\mid x,\bm{z})\log\frac{f_{j}(y=1\mid x,\bm{z})}{f_{0}(y=1\mid x,\bm{z})}+f_{j}(y=-1\mid x,\bm{z})\log\frac{f_{j}(y=-1\mid x,\bm{z})}{f_{0}(y=-1\mid x,\bm{z})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2\sigma}\left(x-\delta_{j}\right)\right)\log\left(1-\frac{\delta_{j}}{\sigma+x}\right)+\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2\sigma}\left(x-\delta_{j}\right)\right)\log\left(1+\frac{\delta_{j}}{\sigma-x}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\delta_{j}^{2}}{2(\sigma+x)(\sigma-x)}+o(\delta_{j}^{2})\leq\delta_{j}^{2}.

Plugging this back to (
A.37
), we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}\left(f^{j}\|f^{0}\right)\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\delta_{j}^{2}\mathbb{P}\left(R_{i}=1\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i},\bar{H}_{i-1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c^{2}\frac{s\log(d/s)}{N}\frac{\bm{\omega}_{j}^{T}}{s}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[Q_{i}\bm{Z}_{i}\bm{Z}_{i}^{T}\right]\bm{\omega}_{j}.

(A.38)

Since
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{N}(\mathcal{P}(\beta,s))
, we have

\bm{\omega}_{j}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{Z}_{i}\bm{Z}_{i}^{T}\cdot\mathbb{P}\left(R_{i}=1\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i},\bar{H}_{i-1}\right)\right]\bm{\omega}_{j}\leq C\|\bm{\omega}_{j}\|_{2}^{2}N.

Giving this back to (
A.38
), and choosing
c
in (
A.28
) sufficiently small, we can ensure

C_{2}
small enough such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}\left(f^{j}\|f^{0}\right)\leq C_{2}\frac{s\log(d/s)}{N}\frac{\|\bm{\omega}_{j}\|_{2}^{2}N}{s}=C_{2}s\log(d/s)\leq\gamma c^{\prime}s\log(d/s)\leq\gamma\log|\mathcal{H}^{\prime}|

for some
\gamma\in(0,1/8)
, hence condition 1 is satisfied.

For condition 2, by Lemma
A.3
, we have when
j\neq 0
,

\left\|\bm{\theta}_{0}-\bm{\theta}_{j}\right\|_{2}=c\left(\frac{s\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{1/2}\left\|\bm{\omega}_{j}\right\|_{2}/\sqrt{s}=c\left(\frac{s\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{1/2},

\left\|\bm{\theta}_{0}-\bm{\theta}_{j}\right\|_{1}=c\left(\frac{s\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{1/2}\left\|\bm{\omega}_{j}\right\|_{1}/\sqrt{s}=c\sqrt{s}\left(\frac{s\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{1/2}.

For all
j,k\neq 0
, we have

\left\|\bm{\theta}_{j}-\bm{\theta}_{k}\right\|_{2}=c\left(\frac{s\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{1/2}\left\|\bm{\omega}_{j}-\bm{\omega}_{k}\right\|_{2}/\sqrt{s}\geq\frac{c}{4}\left(\frac{s\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{1/2},

\left\|\bm{\theta}_{j}-\bm{\theta}_{k}\right\|_{1}=c\left(\frac{s\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{1/2}\left\|\bm{\omega}_{j}-\bm{\omega}_{k}\right\|_{1}/\sqrt{s}\geq\frac{c\sqrt{s}}{16}\left(\frac{s\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{1/2}.

Therefore condition 2 holds. Then we apply Theorem 2.7 in
Tsybakov (2008)
and finish the proof for the case
\beta>1
.

Next we consider
0<\beta\leq 1
. Define
\phi(x)=\operatorname{sign}(x)|x|^{\beta}
for
x\in\mathbb{R}
, which is an odd function. Let
\widetilde{\bm{\omega}}_{j}=s^{\frac{1}{2}(\frac{1}{\beta}-1)}\bm{w}_{j}
, where
\bm{w}_{j}
is the element in
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
. Then,
||\widetilde{\bm{\omega}}_{j}||_{0}=s,||\widetilde{\bm{\omega}}_{j}||_{1}=s^{\frac{1}{2}(\frac{1}{\beta}+1)}
and
||\widetilde{\bm{\omega}}_{j}||_{2}=s^{\frac{1}{2\beta}}
. We construct
P_{j}(X,Y,\bm{Z})
similarly to the case when
\beta>1
using
\widetilde{\bm{\omega}}_{j}
and function
\phi
. That is, we choose weight functions
\gamma(1)=\gamma(-1)
. For
j=0,\dots,|\mathcal{H}^{\prime}|
, assume
X,\bm{Z}_{1},\dots,\bm{Z}_{d}\sim i.i.d.\ U[-1,1]
. For each
j=0,\dots,|\mathcal{H}^{\prime}|
, let

\displaystyle f_{j}(y=1\mid x,\bm{z})=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2\sigma}\phi(x-\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z}),

(A.39)

\displaystyle f_{j}(y=-1\mid x,\bm{z})=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2\sigma}\phi(x-\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z}),

(A.40)

where
\bm{\theta}_{j}=c\left(\frac{\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{1/(2\beta)}\widetilde{\bm{\omega}}_{j}
for
j\neq 0
,
\bm{\theta}_{0}=\bm{0}
,
c
is some sufficiently small constant and
\sigma
is some sufficiently large constant. Under the assumption that
s(\frac{\log(d/s)}{N})^{1/2}=o(1)
, we can guarantee that
P_{0},\ldots,P_{\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}
are well defined probability measures.
Similar to the argument for the case
\beta>1
, we prove the minimax lower bound by verifying the following three conditions:

•

For any sampling method
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{N}(\mathcal{P}(\beta,s))
and
j=1,\dots,|\mathcal{H}^{\prime}|
,
P_{j}(X,Y,\bm{Z})\subset\mathcal{P}(\beta,s)
.

•

\mathrm{KL}\left(f^{j}\|f^{0}\right)\leq\gamma\log|\mathcal{H}|
for some
\gamma\in(0,1/8)
, where
\mathrm{KL}\left(f^{j}\|f^{0}\right)
is the K-L divergence between probability measures
f^{j}
and
f^{0}
, and
f^{j}
is the probability measure of the random variables
\{O_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
under hypothesis
j
.

•

For all
j\neq k
and
q=1,2,\left\|\bm{\theta}^{*}_{j}-\bm{\theta}^{*}_{k}\right\|_{q}\geq 2t
, where
t\asymp s^{\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{s\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{1/(2\beta)}.

By the construction of
P(X,Y,\bm{Z})
, we have

\displaystyle f_{j}(y,\bm{z})=

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}f_{j}(x,y,\bm{z})dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}f_{j}(y\mid x,\bm{z})f(x)f(\bm{z})dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{y}{2\sigma}\phi(x-\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z})\right)\frac{1}{2^{d+1}}\,dx

(A.41)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{d+1}}\left(1+\frac{y}{2\sigma}G(\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z})\right),

(A.42)

where
G(\delta)=\int_{-1}^{1}\phi(x-\delta)\,dx
. Since
G(-\delta)=\int_{-1}^{1}\phi(x+\delta)\,dx=\int_{1}^{-1}\phi(-u+\delta)\,d(-u)=-\int_{-1}^{1}\phi(u-\delta)\,du=-G(\delta)
,
G(\cdot)
is also an odd function. For marginal distribution, we get

f_{j}(x\mid y,\bm{z})=\frac{f_{j}(y\mid x,\bm{z})f(x)f(\bm{z})}{f(y,\bm{z})}=\frac{\sigma+y\phi(x-\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z})}{2\sigma+yG(\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z})}

(A.43)

belongs to the Hölder class
\mathcal{P}(\beta,L)
. This is because

\left|f_{j}^{(0)}\left(x_{1}\mid y,\bm{z}\right)-f_{j}^{(0)}\left(x_{2}\mid y,\bm{z}\right)\right|=\frac{|\phi(x_{1}-\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z})-\phi(x_{2}-\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z})|}{2\sigma+yG(\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z})}\leq C\frac{|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{\beta}}{\sigma},

given
\sigma
sufficiently large and
G(\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z})=o(1)
which will be shown later, and the inequality is by Lemma
A.6
. Clearly,
p_{\min}=1/2-1/\sigma\leq f_{j}(x\mid y,\bm{z})\leq p_{\max}=1/2+1/\sigma
. Thus,
P_{j}\in\mathcal{P}(\beta,L,p_{\min},p_{\max})
holds.
Now we check the condition (
3.2
), i.e.,

\sup_{\|\bm{v}\|_{0}\leq s^{\prime}}\frac{\bm{v}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{Z}\bm{Z}^{T}\mid Y=y\right)\bm{v}}{\|\bm{v}\|_{2}^{2}}\leq M_{1}.

(A.44)

By (
A.41
) we have

P_{j}(Y=1)=\int_{\bm{Z}}\frac{1}{2^{d+1}}\left(1+\frac{1}{2\sigma}G(\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z})\right)d\bm{z}=\frac{1}{2}=P_{j}(Y=-1),

hence
f_{j}(\bm{z}\mid Y=y)=\frac{1}{2^{d}}(1+\frac{y}{2\sigma}G(\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z}))
.
We have

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{Z}\bm{Z}^{T}\mid Y=y\right)\bm{v}=

\displaystyle\int(\bm{v}^{T}\bm{z})^{2}\frac{1}{2^{d}}(1+\frac{y}{2\sigma}G(\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z}))d\bm{z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{T}\mathbb{E}(\bm{Z}\bm{Z}^{T})\bm{v}+\frac{y}{2\sigma}\int\frac{1}{2^{d}}(\bm{v}^{T}\bm{z})^{2}G(\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z}))d\bm{z}.

Note that
\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{Z}\bm{Z}^{T}\right)=1/3\mathbb{I}_{d}
,
|\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z}|\leq||\bm{\theta}_{j}||_{1}=c\left(\frac{\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\beta}}s^{\frac{1}{2}(\frac{1}{\beta}+1)}\lesssim\left(\frac{\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\beta}}s^{\frac{1}{\beta}}=o(1)
and

\displaystyle|G(\delta)|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\int_{-1}^{1}\phi(x-\delta)\,dx\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{-1-\delta}^{1-\delta}\phi(u)\,du\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{-1-\delta}^{-1}\phi(u)\,du+\int_{1}^{1-\delta}\phi(u)\,du\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\min\{-1-\delta,-1\}}^{\max\{-1-\delta,-1\}}|\phi(u)|\,du+\int_{\min\{1,1-\delta\}}^{\max\{1,1-\delta\}}|\phi(u)|\,du\leq C|\delta|

for some constant
C
if
\delta=o(1)
. Hence, we get

\displaystyle\Big|\int\frac{1}{2^{d}}(\bm{v}^{T}\bm{z})^{2}G(\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z}))d\bm{z}d\bm{z}\Big|\leq C\|\bm{\theta}_{j}\|_{1}\mathbb{E}((\bm{v}^{T}\bm{Z})^{2})\leq C\left(\frac{\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\beta}}s^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\frac{\|\bm{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{3},

and

\displaystyle|\bm{v}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{Z}\bm{Z}^{T}\mid Y=y\right)\bm{v}|\leq\frac{1}{3}\|\bm{v}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{6\sigma}s^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\left(\frac{\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\beta}}\|\bm{v}\|_{2}^{2}.

Since
s^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\left(\frac{\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\beta}}=o(1)
, (
A.44
) holds.
Similar to Lemma
A.3
, we then show the following results: (1)
\bm{\theta}_{j}
is the unique minimizer of
R_{P_{j}}(\bm{\theta})
,
(2)
\|\bm{\theta}_{j}\|_{2}\leq C
for some constant
C>0
, and (3)
\rho_{-}||\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}_{j}||_{2}^{1+\beta}\leq R(\bm{\theta})-R(\bm{\theta}_{j})-\nabla R(\bm{\theta})^{T}(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}_{j})\leq\rho_{+}||\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}_{j}||_{2}^{1+\beta}
for some positive constants
\rho_{-}
and
\rho_{+}
.

In fact, recall that
R_{j}(\bm{\theta})=\mathbb{E}_{j}\left[\gamma(Y)\left(1-\operatorname{sign}\left(Y\left(X-\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{Z}\right)\right)\right)\right]
, and
\gamma(1)=\gamma(-1)=C
for some constant
C>0
,
the difference between
R_{j}(\bm{\theta})
and
R_{j}(\bm{\theta}_{j})
can be written as

\displaystyle R_{j}(\bm{\theta})-R_{j}\left(\bm{\theta}_{j}\right)=

\displaystyle 2C\int_{\mathcal{G}}\operatorname{sign}\left(x-\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z}\right)\mathbb{E}_{j}[Y\mid x,\bm{z}]dP_{j;X,\bm{Z}},

where

\mathcal{G}=\left\{(x,\bm{z})\mid\operatorname{sign}\left(x-\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{z}\right)\neq\operatorname{sign}\left(x-\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z}\right)\right\},

P_{j;X,\bm{Z}}
is the joint distribution of
(X,\bm{Z})
under
P_{j}
, and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{j}[Y\mid x,\bm{z}]=

\displaystyle f_{j}(Y=1\mid x,\bm{z})-f_{j}(Y=-1\mid x,\bm{z})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sigma}\phi(x-\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z}),

(A.45)

\operatorname{sign}\left(\mathbb{E}_{j}[Y\mid x,\bm{z}]\right)=\operatorname{sign}\left(\phi(x-\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z})\right)=\operatorname{sign}\left(x-\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z}\right).

Therefore,

R_{j}(\bm{\theta})-R_{j}\left(\bm{\theta}_{j}\right)=2C\int_{\mathcal{G}}\left|\mathbb{E}_{j}[Y\mid x,\bm{z}]\right|dP_{j;X,\bm{Z}}\geq 0,

(A.46)

hence
\bm{\theta}_{j}
is a minimizer of
R_{j}(\bm{\theta})
. In addition,
\|\bm{\theta}_{j}\|_{2}=c(\frac{\log(d/s)}{N})^{\frac{1}{2\beta}}\|\widetilde{\bm{\omega}}_{j}\|_{2}=c(\frac{s\log(d/s)}{N})^{\frac{1}{2\beta}}=O(1)
.

Now we check the uniqueness. By (
A.45
) we have

R_{j}(\bm{\theta})-R_{j}\left(\bm{\theta}_{j}\right)=\frac{2C}{\sigma}\int_{\mathcal{G}}\left|\phi(x-\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z})\right|dP_{j;X,\bm{Z}}.

(A.47)

For any
\bm{\theta}\neq\bm{\theta}_{j}
, consider the set

\mathcal{G}_{\bm{z}}=\left\{\bm{z}:\left(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}_{j}\right)^{T}\bm{z}\neq 0,\left|\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{z}\right|\leq 1,\left|\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z}\right|\leq 1\right\}.

Note that there exists an open neighborhood in
\mathcal{G}_{\bm{z}}
, hence
\mathcal{G}_{\bm{z}}
has nonzero measure. Then
define

\bar{\mathcal{G}}=\left\{(x,\bm{z}):\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{z}<x<\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z}\right.
or
\left.\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z}<x<\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{z},\bm{z}\in\mathcal{G}_{\bm{z}}\right\}
, we have
\bar{\mathcal{G}}\subset\mathcal{G}_{\bm{z}}
and
\bar{\mathcal{G}}
has nonzero measure as well. Therefore, (
A.47
) implies that
R_{j}(\bm{\theta})-R_{j}\left(\bm{\theta}_{j}\right)>0
for
\bm{\theta}\neq\bm{\theta}_{j}
, which completes the proof of the uniqueness.

Finally, Lemma
A.4
and Lemma
A.5
in below implies that
\rho_{-}||\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}||_{2}^{1+\beta}\leq R(\bm{\theta})-R(\bm{\theta}^{*})-\nabla R(\bm{\theta}^{*})^{T}(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*})\leq\rho_{+}||\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}||_{2}^{1+\beta}
for some positive constants
\rho_{-}
and
\rho_{+}
.

Next, we verify the KL condition using the similar argument in the proof for the case
\beta>1
. For
f^{j}
, the probability measure of the random variables
\{(X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i},Y_{i},R_{i})^{\ind\{R_{i}=1\}},(X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i},R_{i})^{\ind\{R_{i}=0\}}\}_{i=1}^{n}
under hypothesis
j
, and
\bar{H}_{i-1}=\{(X_{j},\bm{Z}_{j},Y_{j},R_{j})^{\ind\{R_{j}=1\}},(X_{j},\bm{Z}_{j},R_{j})^{\ind\{R_{j}=0\}}\}_{j=1}^{i-1}
, we can still get

\displaystyle f^{j}=

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left\{f(R_{i}=1\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i},\bar{H}_{i-1})\right\}^{\ind\{R_{i}=1\}}\left\{f(R_{i}=0\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i},\bar{H}_{i-1})\right\}^{\ind\{R_{i}=0\}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \cdot f_{j}(Y_{i}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i})^{\ind\{R_{i}=1\}}f_{j}(X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i}),

and

\mathrm{KL}\left(f^{j}\|f^{0}\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\log\frac{f_{j}(Y_{i}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i})}{f_{0}(Y_{i}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i})}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i}\right]\cdot\mathbb{P}\left(R_{i}=1\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i},\bar{H}_{i-1}\right)\right].

(A.48)

Note that
\mathbb{E}\left[\log\frac{f_{j}(Y_{i}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i})}{f_{0}(Y_{i}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i})}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i}\right]
is the K-L divergence between two Bernoulli distributions
f_{j}(Y_{i}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i})
and
f_{0}(Y_{i}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i})
. Denote
p_{j}=f_{j}(y=1\mid x,\bm{z}),\Delta_{j}=p_{j}-p_{0}=\frac{1}{2\sigma}\left(\phi(x-\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z})-\phi(x)\right)
, then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\log\frac{f_{j}(Y_{i}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i})}{f_{0}(Y_{i}\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i})}\mid X_{i}=x,\bm{Z}_{i}=\bm{z}\right]

(A.49)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p_{j}\log\frac{p_{j}}{p_{0}}+(1-p_{j})\log\frac{1-p_{j}}{1-p_{0}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(p_{0}+\Delta_{j})\left(\frac{\Delta_{j}}{p_{0}}-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\Delta_{j}}{p_{0}}\right)^{2}+o(\Delta_{j}^{2})\right)+(1-p_{0}-\Delta_{j})\left(-\frac{\Delta_{j}}{1-p_{0}}-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\Delta_{j}}{1-p_{0}}\right)^{2}+o(\Delta_{j}^{2})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta_{j}^{2}}{2p_{0}(1-p_{0})}+o(\Delta_{j}^{2})=\frac{\left(\phi(x-\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z})-\phi(x)\right)^{2}}{2(\sigma^{2}-\phi(x)^{2})}+o((\phi(x-\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z})-\phi(x))^{2}).

Since
|\phi(x-\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z})-\phi(x)|\leq C|\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z}|^{\beta}
by Lemma
A.6
, (
A.49
)
\leq C^{\prime}|\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z}|^{2\beta}
for some constant
C^{\prime}>0
. Plugging this back to (
A.48
), we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}\left(f^{j}\|f^{0}\right)\leq

\displaystyle C^{\prime}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[|\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z}|^{2\beta}\mathbb{P}\left(R_{i}=1\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i},\bar{H}_{i-1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C^{\prime}\frac{\log(d/s)}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[Q_{i}|\widetilde{\bm{\omega}}_{j}^{T}\bm{Z}_{i}|^{2\beta}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C^{\prime}\frac{\log(d/s)}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[Q_{i}^{1-\beta}Q_{i}^{\beta}|\widetilde{\bm{\omega}}_{j}^{T}\bm{Z}_{i}|^{2\beta}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C^{\prime}\frac{\log(d/s)}{N}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}Q_{i}\right)^{1-\beta}\left(\widetilde{\bm{\omega}}_{j}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}(Q_{i}\bm{Z}_{i}\bm{Z}_{i}^{T})\widetilde{\bm{\omega}}_{j}\right)^{\beta}

\displaystyle\text{(By H\"{o}lder's Inequality)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C^{\prime}\frac{\log(d/s)}{N}N^{1-\beta}\left(C_{2}||\widetilde{\bm{\omega}}_{j}||_{2}^{2}N\right)^{\beta}

\displaystyle(Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{N}(\mathcal{P}(\beta,s)))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C^{\prime}\frac{\log(d/s)}{N}Ns=C^{\prime}s\log(d/s)\leq\gamma\log|\mathcal{H}^{\prime}|

for some
\gamma\in(0,1/8)
, hence the KL condition is satisfied.
Finally, we verify the last condition that for all
j\neq k
and
q=1,2,\left\|\bm{\theta}_{j}-\bm{\theta}_{k}\right\|_{q}\gtrsim s^{\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{s\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{1/(2\beta)}
. By definition of
\bm{\theta}_{j}
, we have when
j\neq 0
,

\left\|\bm{\theta}_{0}-\bm{\theta}_{j}\right\|_{2}\gtrsim\left(\frac{\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\beta}}||\widetilde{\bm{\omega}}_{j}||_{2}=\left(\frac{\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\beta}}s^{\frac{1}{2\beta}}=\left(\frac{s\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\beta}},

\left\|\bm{\theta}_{0}-\bm{\theta}_{j}\right\|_{1}\gtrsim\left(\frac{\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\beta}}||\widetilde{\bm{\omega}}_{j}||_{1}=\left(\frac{\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\beta}}s^{\frac{1}{2}(\frac{1}{\beta}+1)}=\sqrt{s}\left(\frac{s\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\beta}}.

For all
j,k\neq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\theta}_{j}-\bm{\theta}_{k}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\left(\frac{\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\beta}}||\widetilde{\bm{\omega}}_{j}-\widetilde{\bm{\omega}}_{k}||_{2}

(A.50)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\beta}}s^{\frac{1}{2}(\frac{1}{\beta}-1)}||\bm{\omega}_{j}-\bm{\omega}_{k}||_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\beta}}s^{\frac{1}{2}(\frac{1}{\beta}-1)}\frac{\sqrt{s}}{4}\gtrsim\left(\frac{s\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\beta}},

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\theta}_{j}-\bm{\theta}_{k}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\left(\frac{\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\beta}}||\widetilde{\bm{\omega}}_{j}-\widetilde{\bm{\omega}}_{k}||_{1}

(A.51)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\beta}}s^{\frac{1}{2}(\frac{1}{\beta}-1)}||\bm{\omega}_{j}-\bm{\omega}_{k}||_{1}

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\beta}}s^{\frac{1}{2}(\frac{1}{\beta}-1)}\frac{s}{16}\gtrsim\sqrt{s}\left(\frac{s\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\beta}},

which completes the proof for the case
0<\beta\leq 1
.
∎

Lemma A.4
.

Under Assumption
3.2
(iii), if we further assume
\inf\limits_{||\bm{u}||_{2}=1}\mathbb{E}[|\bm{u}^{T}\bm{Z}|^{1+\beta}\mid Y=y]\geq c
for some constant
c>0
,

L_{1}|\Delta|^{\beta}\leq\operatorname{sign}(\Delta)\left(f(\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{z}+\Delta\mid\bm{z},y=1)-f(\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{z}\mid\bm{z},y=1)\right)\leq L_{2}|\Delta|^{\beta}

(A.52)

and

L_{1}|\Delta|^{\beta}\leq-\operatorname{sign}(\Delta)\left(f(\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{z}+\Delta\mid\bm{z},y=-1)-f(\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{z}\mid\bm{z},y=-1)\right)\leq L_{2}|\Delta|^{\beta}

(A.53)

for any
|\Delta|\leq\sqrt{s}M_{n}r_{n}
, then we have

\rho_{-}||\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}||_{2}^{1+\beta}\leq R(\bm{\theta})-R(\bm{\theta}^{*})-\nabla R(\bm{\theta}^{*})^{T}(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*})\leq\rho_{+}||\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}||_{2}^{1+\beta}

for
||\theta-\theta^{*}||_{2}\leq r_{n}
, where
\rho_{-}
and
\rho_{+}
are positive constants and
r_{n}
goes to 0 fast enough as (
3.25
).

Proof.

We take
||\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}||_{2}\leq r_{n}
, then
|(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*})^{T}\bm{z}|\leq||\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}||_{1}M_{n}\leq\sqrt{s}||\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}||_{2}M_{n}\leq\sqrt{s}M_{n}r_{n}
.
By definition

\displaystyle R(\bm{\theta})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\gamma(Y)L_{01}\{Y(X-\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{Z})\}\right]

(A.54)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[L_{01}\{X-\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{Z}\}\mid Y=1\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[L_{01}\{-X+\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{Z}\}\mid Y=-1\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(X<\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{Z}\mid Y=1)+\mathbb{P}(X>\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{Z}\mid Y=-1)

\displaystyle=\int\int_{-\infty}^{\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{z}}f(x\mid\bm{z},y=1)\ dxf(\bm{z}|y=1)\ d\bm{z}+\int\int_{\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{z}}^{\infty}f(x\mid\bm{z},y=-1)\ dxf(\bm{z}|y=-1)\ d\bm{z}.

Hence,

\nabla R(\bm{\theta})=\int\bm{z}f(\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{z}\mid\bm{z},y=1)f(\bm{z}\mid y=1)\ d\bm{z}-\int\bm{z}f(\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{z}\mid\bm{z},y=-1)f(\bm{z}\mid y=-1)\ d\bm{z},

\displaystyle R(\bm{\theta})-R(\bm{\theta}^{*})-\nabla R(\bm{\theta}^{*})^{T}(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*})

(A.55)

\displaystyle=\int\int_{0}^{\Delta}f(\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{z}+t\mid\bm{z},y=1)\ dtf(\bm{z}\mid y=1)\ d\bm{z}-\int\int_{0}^{\Delta}f(\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{z}+t\mid\bm{z},y=-1)\ dtf(\bm{z}\mid y=-1)\ d\bm{z}

\displaystyle-\int(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*})^{T}\bm{z}f(\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{z}\mid\bm{z},y=1)f(\bm{z}\mid y=1)\ d\bm{z}+\int(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*})^{T}\bm{z}f(\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{z}\mid\bm{z},y=-1)f(\bm{z}\mid y=-1)\ d\bm{z}

\displaystyle=\int\int_{0}^{\Delta}\left[f(\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{z}+t\mid\bm{z},y=1)-f(\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{z}\mid\bm{z},y=1)\right]\ dtf(\bm{z}\mid y=1)\ d\bm{z}

\displaystyle-\int\int_{0}^{\Delta}\left[f(\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{z}+t\mid\bm{z},y=-1)-f(\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{z}\mid\bm{z},y=-1)\right]\ dtf(\bm{z}\mid y=-1)\ d\bm{z}

\displaystyle=\int\int_{0}^{\Delta}\left[f(\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{z}+t\mid\bm{z},y=1)-f(\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{z}\mid\bm{z},y=1)\right](I(\Delta>0)+I(\Delta\leq 0))\ dtf(\bm{z}\mid y=1)\ d\bm{z}

\displaystyle-\int\int_{0}^{\Delta}\left[f(\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{z}+t\mid\bm{z},y=-1)-f(\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{z}\mid\bm{z},y=-1)\right](I(\Delta>0)+I(\Delta\leq 0))\ dtf(\bm{z}\mid y=-1)\ d\bm{z},

where
\Delta=(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*})^{T}\bm{z}
. By (
A.52
) and (
A.53
),

\displaystyle\text{(\ref{eq: Taylor remainder})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{L_{2}}{1+\beta}\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta^{1+\beta}I(\Delta>0)+(-\Delta)^{1+\beta}I(\Delta\leq 0)\mid Y=1\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{L_{2}}{1+\beta}\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta^{1+\beta}I(\Delta>0)+(-\Delta)^{1+\beta}I(\Delta\leq 0)\mid Y=-1\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{L_{2}}{1+\beta}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta|^{1+\beta}\mid Y=1\right]+\frac{L_{2}}{1+\beta}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta|^{1+\beta}\mid Y=-1\right].

Since
\bm{Z}\mid Y=y
is a sub-Gaussian vector with a bounded sub-Gaussian norm, denoted as
K_{y}
,
(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*})^{T}\bm{Z}\mid Y=y
is also a sub-Gaussian random variable with the sub-Gaussian norm
||\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}||_{2}K_{y}
. Then
\mathbb{E}\left[|(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*})^{T}\bm{Z}|^{1+\beta}\mid Y=y\right]\leq C_{\beta}K_{y}^{1+\beta}||\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}||_{2}^{1+\beta}
for some constant
C_{\beta}>0
. Hence
(\text{\ref{eq: Taylor remainder}})\leq\rho_{+}||\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}||_{2}^{1+\beta}
for some constant
\rho_{+}
.
Similarly, we can get the lower bound

\text{(\ref{eq: Taylor remainder})}\geq\frac{L_{1}}{1+\beta}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta|^{1+\beta}\mid Y=1\right]+\frac{L_{1}}{1+\beta}\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta|^{1+\beta}\mid Y=-1\right].

Notice that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta|^{1+\beta}\mid Y=y\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[|(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*})^{T}\bm{z}|^{1+\beta}\mid Y=y]

(A.56)

\displaystyle=||\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}||_{2}^{1+\beta}\mathbb{E}[|\bm{u}^{T}\bm{z}|^{1+\beta}\mid Y=y]

\displaystyle\geq c||\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}||_{2}^{1+\beta},

where
\bm{u}=\frac{\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}}{||\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}||_{2}}
. We also have
(\text{\ref{eq: Taylor remainder}})\geq\rho_{-}||\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}||_{2}^{1+\beta}
for some constant
\rho_{-}
.
∎

Lemma A.5
.

Suppose
0<\beta\leq 1
. Let
\bm{Z}=(Z_{1},\dots,Z_{d})
where
\{Z_{i}\}_{i=1}^{d}
are i.i.d. and uniformly distributed on
[-1,1]
.
f_{j}(\bm{z}\mid Y=y)=\frac{1}{2^{d}}(1+\frac{y}{2\sigma}G(\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z}))
, where the
G(\delta)=\int_{-1}^{1}\mathop{\mathrm{sign}}(x-\delta)|x-\delta|^{\beta}\ dx
and
\sigma
,
\bm{\theta}_{j}
are defined in (
A.39
). Then
\inf\limits_{||\bm{u}||_{2}=1}\mathbb{E}[|\bm{u}^{T}\bm{Z}|^{1+\beta}\mid Y=y]\geq c
for some constant
c>0
.

Proof.

We first prove that
\inf\limits_{||\bm{u}||_{2}=1}\mathbb{E}[|\bm{u}\bm{Z}|^{1+\beta}]\geq\frac{1}{3}
. Denote
S=\bm{u}^{T}\bm{Z}=\sum_{i=1}^{d}u_{i}Z_{i}
for
||\bm{u}||_{2}=1
. If
\beta=1
, then

\mathbb{E}[|\bm{u}^{T}\bm{Z}|^{2}]=\mathbb{E}(\sum_{i=1}^{d}u_{i}^{2}Z_{i}^{2}+\sum_{i\neq j}u_{i}u_{j}Z_{i}Z_{j})=\sum_{i=1}^{d}u_{i}^{2}\mathbb{E}Z_{i}^{2}=\frac{1}{3}\sum_{i=1}^{d}u_{i}^{2}=\frac{1}{3}.

For
0<\beta<1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}S^{4}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}(\sum_{i=1}^{d}u_{i}Z_{i})^{4}=\sum_{i=1}^{d}u_{i}^{4}\mathbb{E}Z_{i}^{4}+3\sum_{i\neq j}u_{i}^{2}u_{j}^{2}\mathbb{E}Z_{i}^{2}\mathbb{E}Z_{j}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{5}\sum_{i=1}^{d}u_{i}^{4}+\frac{1}{3}\sum_{i\neq j}u_{i}^{2}u_{j}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{5}\sum_{i=1}^{d}u_{i}^{4}+\frac{1}{3}\left((\sum_{i=1}^{d}u_{i}^{2})^{2}-\sum_{i=1}^{d}u_{i}^{4}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}(\sum_{i=1}^{d}u_{i}^{2})^{2}-\frac{2}{15}\sum_{i=1}^{d}u_{i}^{4}\leq\frac{1}{3}(\sum_{i=1}^{d}u_{i}^{2})^{2}=\frac{1}{3}.

Since
1+\beta<2<4
, we can write
2=p(1+\beta)+(1-p)4
for some
0<p<1
. Then by Hölder’s inequality,

\mathbb{E}S^{2}=\mathbb{E}\left[|S|^{p(1+\beta)}|S|^{4(1-p)}\right]\leq(\mathbb{E}|S|^{1+\beta})^{p}(\mathbb{E}S^{4})^{1-p}.

Hence,

(\mathbb{E}|S|^{1+\beta})^{p}\geq\frac{\mathbb{E}S^{2}}{(\mathbb{E}S^{4})^{1-p}}\geq\frac{\frac{1}{3}}{(\frac{1}{3})^{1-p}}=(\frac{1}{3})^{p},

which implies
\inf\limits_{||\bm{u}||_{2}=1}\mathbb{E}[|\bm{u}^{T}\bm{Z}|^{1+\beta}]\geq\frac{1}{3}
for
0<\beta<1
. Now we prove the lower bound of conditional expectation.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\bm{u}^{T}\bm{Z}|^{1+\beta}\mid Y=y]

\displaystyle=\int|\bm{u}^{T}\bm{z}|^{1+\beta}f_{j}(\bm{z}\mid Y=y)\ d\bm{z}

\displaystyle=\int|\bm{u}^{T}\bm{z}|^{1+\beta}f(\bm{z})\left[1+\frac{y}{2\sigma}G(\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z})\right]\ d\bm{z}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[|\bm{u}^{T}\bm{Z}|^{1+\beta}]+\frac{y}{2\sigma}\mathbb{E}\left[|\bm{u}^{T}\bm{Z}|^{1+\beta}G(\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{Z})\right].

We have shown the first term
\mathbb{E}[|\bm{u}^{T}\bm{Z}|^{1+\beta}]\geq\frac{1}{3}
. Then it is enough to show the second term is
o(1)
. Note that we proved
|\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{z}|\lesssim\left(\frac{\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\beta}}s^{\frac{1}{\beta}}=o(1)
and
|G(\delta)|\leq C|\delta|
for some constant
C
if
\delta=o(1)
in the proof of Theorem
5
. Hence,
|G(\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{Z})|\leq C|\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{Z}|\leq C^{\prime}\left(\frac{\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\beta}}s^{\frac{1}{\beta}}
, and

\displaystyle\big|\frac{y}{2\sigma}\mathbb{E}\left[|\bm{u}^{T}\bm{Z}|^{1+\beta}G(\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{Z})\right]\big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2\sigma}\mathbb{E}\left[|\bm{u}^{T}\bm{Z}|^{1+\beta}|G(\bm{\theta}_{j}^{T}\bm{Z})|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{2\sigma}\left(\frac{\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\beta}}s^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\mathbb{E}\left[|\bm{u}^{T}\bm{Z}|^{1+\beta}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{2\sigma}\left(\frac{\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\beta}}s^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\left(\mathbb{E}|\bm{u}^{T}\bm{Z}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1+\beta}{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{C^{\prime}}{2\sigma}\left(\frac{\log(d/s)}{N}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\beta}}s^{\frac{1}{\beta}}(\frac{1}{3})^{\frac{1+\beta}{2}}=o(1),

where the third inequality is due to Hölder’s inequality. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma A.6
.

Suppose
0<\beta<1
. For
\phi(x)=\mathop{\mathrm{sign}}(x)|x|^{\beta}
for
-1\leq x\leq 1
, we have the Hölder condition:

|\phi(x)-\phi(y)|\leq c|x-y|^{\beta}

(A.57)

for any
x,y\in[-1,1]
, and
c
is a positive constant. Furthermore, let
g(x)=\phi(x-a)
for some
a\in\mathbb{R}
. Then
|g(a+\Delta)-g(a)|\geq|\Delta|^{\beta}
.

Proof.

For
0<\beta<1
, we first recall that the map
t\mapsto t^{\beta}
is concave on
[0,\infty)
and satisfies
t^{\beta}\geq t
for all
t\in[0,1]
. And by Jensen’s inequality, for any
a,b\in[0,1]
,
\frac{a^{\beta}+b^{\beta}}{2}\leq\Big(\frac{a+b}{2}\Big)^{\beta}
, which leads to

a^{\beta}+b^{\beta}\leq 2^{1-\beta}(a+b)^{\beta}.

(A.58)

Now fix
x,y\in(-1,1)
and set
\phi(x)=\operatorname{sign}(x)|x|^{\beta}
.
We distinguish three cases.

First we consider
x,y\geq 0
.
Without loss of generality, we assume
x\geq y\geq 0
. Let
y=tx
for some
t\in[0,1]
. Then (
A.57
) is equivalent to
1-t^{\beta}\leq c(1-t)^{\beta}
. Since
t^{\beta}\geq t
and
(1-t)^{\beta}\geq 1-t
, this holds for
c=1
.

Then if
x,y\leq 0
, we have

\displaystyle|\phi(x)-\phi(y)|

\displaystyle=\big|\mathop{\mathrm{sign}}(x)|x|^{\beta}-\mathop{\mathrm{sign}}(y)|y|^{\beta}\big|

(A.59)

\displaystyle=\big||x|^{\beta}-|y|^{\beta}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big||x|-|y|\big|^{\beta}~~(\text{By conclusion in first case})

\displaystyle\leq|x-y|^{\beta}.

Finally, if
\mathop{\mathrm{sign}}(x)\neq\mathop{\mathrm{sign}}(y)
, we assume
x\geq 0\geq y
without loss of generality. Then

|\phi(x)-\phi(y)|=x^{\beta}+(-y)^{\beta}\leq 2^{1-\beta}(x-y)^{\beta},

where the last inequality is from (
A.58
). Combining the three cases, we obtain

|\phi(x)-\phi(y)|\leq 2^{1-\beta}|x-y|^{\beta},\qquad\forall x,y\in(-1,1),

which completes the proof. And the conclusion for function
g(\cdot)
is trivial.
∎

A.6
Supplementary Theoretical Results

A.6.1
Extension to unbounded kernel and relaxed Assumption
3.2

In this section, we assume that there exits a sequence
C_{N}>0
that may depend on
N
such that

\int_{C_{N}/2}^{\infty}|K(t)|dt\leq C\delta_{k}^{\beta},

(A.60)

holds for any
2\leq k\leq K
, where
C
is a constant that does not depend on
k
and
\delta_{k}
is the bandwidth parameter in the
k
th iteration. In addition, we also remove the sub-Gaussian vector assumption for
\bm{Z}
in Assumption
3.2
in this section. However, in this case we need the following stronger version of the smoothness condition,

\left|f^{(l)}\left(\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{z}+\Delta\mid y,\bm{z}\right)-f^{(l)}\left(\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{z}\mid y,\bm{z}\right)\right|\leq L\left|\Delta\right|^{\beta-l},

(A.61)

for any
\Delta\in\mathbb{R},y\in\{-1,1\},\bm{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and some constant
L>0
. Compared to (
3.3
), the above inequality holds for any
\Delta\in\mathbb{R}
.

The proof of the following theorems is similar to the proof of the main results in the previous section, and is omitted to avoid repetition. We note that without the sub-Gaussian vector assumption, it becomes complicated to pinpoint the critical points for
\beta
at which the transition of the property of the algorithm occurs. Indeed, the conditions (
A.62
), (
A.65
) and (
A.69
) in the following three theorems correspond to the cases (i)
\beta\in((1+\sqrt{3})/2,+\infty)
; (ii)
\beta\in(1,(1+\sqrt{3})/2]
; and (iii)
\beta=1
considered in the main paper.

Theorem 6
.

Assume that Assumptions
3.1
,
3.2
(i) and (ii),
3.3
-
3.5
hold, and
K\geq 2
. We set
N_{k}=N/K
for
1\leq k\leq K
, and

\delta_{1}=c_{1}\left(\frac{Ks\log d}{N}\right)^{1/(2\beta+1)},\ \lambda_{1}=c_{2}\sqrt{\frac{NK\log d}{n^{2}\delta_{1}}},

\delta_{k}=c_{1}\left(\frac{C_{N}Ks\log d}{N}\right)^{1/(2\beta)},\ \lambda_{k}=c_{2}\sqrt{\frac{NK\log d}{n^{2}b_{k-1}\delta_{k}}},\ b_{k-1}=c_{3}\left(\frac{C_{N}^{2\beta+1}Ks\log d}{N}\right)^{1/(2\beta)},2\leq k\leq K,

for some constants
c_{1},c_{2},c_{3}>0
and
c_{3}\geq c_{1}
. If

\displaystyle\sqrt{s}M_{n}\left(\left(\frac{Ks\log d}{N}\right)^{\beta/(2\beta+1)}\vee\left(\frac{C_{N}Ks\log d}{N}\right)^{1/2}\right)=O\left(\left(\frac{C_{N}^{2\beta+1}Ks\log d}{N}\right)^{1/(2\beta)}\right),

(A.62)

and

N\leq Cn^{2\beta/(2\beta+1)}(Ks\log d)^{1/(2\beta+1)}C_{N}

(A.63)

hold for some constant
C
, then with probability greater than
1-2K/d
, we have

\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{K}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}\lesssim\left(\frac{C_{N}Ks\log d}{N}\right)^{1/2},\ \|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{K}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{1}\lesssim\sqrt{s}\left(\frac{C_{N}Ks\log d}{N}\right)^{1/2},

(A.64)

where
N
is the given label budget.

Theorem 7
.

Assume that Assumptions
3.1
,
3.2
(i) and (ii),
3.3
-
3.5
hold, and
suppose there exists an integer
K^{*}
such that

\frac{M_{n}^{\frac{2\beta+1}{\beta+1}-\frac{\beta^{K^{*}-1}}{(\beta+1)(2\beta+1)^{K^{*}-2}}}s^{\frac{2\beta+1}{2(\beta+1)}-\frac{\beta^{K^{*}-1}}{2(\beta+1)(2\beta+1)^{K^{*}-2}}}}{C_{N}^{(2\beta+1)/(2\beta)}}=O\left(\left(\frac{K\log d}{N}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\beta}-\frac{\beta}{\beta+1}+\frac{\beta^{K^{*}}}{(\beta+1)(2\beta+1)^{K^{*}-1}}}\right).

(A.65)

We set
K=K^{*}
,
N_{k}=N/K
for
1\leq k\leq K
. We set

\delta_{1}=c_{1}\left(\frac{sK\log d}{N}\right)^{1/(2\beta+1)},\ \lambda_{1}=c_{2}\sqrt{\frac{NK\log d}{n^{2}\delta_{1}}},

for
2\leq k\leq K-1
,

\delta_{k}=c_{1}\left(\frac{Ks\log db_{k-1}}{N}\right)^{1/(2\beta+1)},~\lambda_{k}=c_{2}\sqrt{\frac{NK\log d}{n^{2}b_{k-1}\delta_{k}}},

b_{k-1}=c_{3}M_{n}^{\frac{2\beta+1}{\beta+1}-\frac{\beta^{k-1}}{(\beta+1)(2\beta+1)^{k-2}}}s^{\frac{2\beta+1}{2(\beta+1)}-\frac{\beta^{k-1}}{2(\beta+1)(2\beta+1)^{k-2}}}\left(\frac{Ks\log d}{N}\right)^{\frac{\beta}{\beta+1}-\frac{\beta^{k}}{(\beta+1)(2\beta+1)^{k-1}}},

for some constants
c_{1},c_{2},c_{3}>0
, and

\delta_{K}=c_{1}^{\prime}\left(\frac{C_{N}Ks\log d}{N}\right)^{1/(2\beta)},\ \lambda_{K}=c_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{\frac{NK\log d}{n^{2}b_{K-1}\delta_{K}}},\ b_{K-1}=c_{3}^{\prime}\left(\frac{C_{N}^{2\beta+1}Ks\log d}{N}\right)^{1/(2\beta)},

for some constants
c_{1}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime},c_{3}^{\prime}>0
and
c_{3}^{\prime}\geq c_{1}^{\prime}
. If

\left(\frac{K\log d}{N}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\beta}-\frac{\beta}{\beta+1}+\frac{\beta^{k}}{(\beta+1)(2\beta+1)^{k-1}}}=O\left(\frac{M_{n}^{\frac{2\beta+1}{\beta+1}-\frac{\beta^{k-1}}{(\beta+1)(2\beta+1)^{k-2}}}s^{\frac{2\beta+1}{2(\beta+1)}-\frac{\beta^{k-1}}{2(\beta+1)(2\beta+1)^{k-2}}}}{C_{N}^{(2\beta+1)/(2\beta)}}\right)

(A.66)

for
2\leq k\leq K-1
,
and

N\leq C_{1}M_{n}^{\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+1}}s^{\frac{\beta+1}{2(2\beta+1)}}(Ks\log d)^{\frac{\beta}{2\beta+1}},

(A.67)

N\leq C_{2}n^{2\beta/(2\beta+1)}(Ks\log d)^{1/(2\beta+1)}C_{N}

(A.68)

hold for some constants
C_{1},C_{2}
, then with probability greater than
1-2Kd^{-1}
,

\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{K}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}\lesssim\left(\frac{C_{N}Ks\log d}{N}\right)^{1/2},\ \|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{K}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{1}\lesssim\sqrt{s}\left(\frac{C_{N}Ks\log d}{N}\right)^{1/2}.

Theorem 8
.

Assume that Assumptions
3.1
,
3.2
(i) and (ii),
3.3
-
3.5
hold. We set
N_{k}=N/K
for
1\leq k\leq K
, and

\delta_{1}=c_{1}\left(\frac{Ks\log d}{N}\right)^{1/(2\beta+1)},\ \lambda_{1}=c_{2}\sqrt{\frac{NK\log d}{n^{2}\delta_{1}}},

\ 2\leq k\leq K:\ \delta_{k}=c_{1}\left(\frac{Ks\log db_{k-1}}{N}\right)^{1/(2\beta+1)},~\lambda_{k}=c_{2}\sqrt{\frac{NK\log d}{n^{2}b_{k-1}\delta_{k}}},

b_{k-1}=c_{3}M_{n}^{\frac{2\beta+1}{\beta+1}-\frac{\beta^{k-1}}{(\beta+1)(2\beta+1)^{k-2}}}s^{\frac{2\beta+1}{2(\beta+1)}-\frac{\beta^{k-1}}{2(\beta+1)(2\beta+1)^{k-2}}}\left(\frac{Ks\log d}{N}\right)^{\frac{\beta}{\beta+1}-\frac{\beta^{k}}{(\beta+1)(2\beta+1)^{k-1}}},

for some constants
c_{1},c_{2},c_{3}>0
. If

\left(\frac{K\log d}{N}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\beta}-\frac{\beta}{\beta+1}+\frac{\beta^{k}}{(\beta+1)(2\beta+1)^{k-1}}}=O\left(\frac{M_{n}^{\frac{2\beta+1}{\beta+1}-\frac{\beta^{k-1}}{(\beta+1)(2\beta+1)^{k-2}}}s^{\frac{2\beta+1}{2(\beta+1)}-\frac{\beta^{k-1}}{2(\beta+1)(2\beta+1)^{k-2}}}}{C_{N}^{(2\beta+1)/(2\beta)}}\right)

(A.69)

for all
2\leq k\leq K
,
and

N\leq CM_{n}^{\frac{\beta+1}{2\beta+1}}s^{\frac{\beta+1}{2(2\beta+1)}}(Ks\log d)^{\frac{\beta}{2\beta+1}},

(A.70)

hold for some constant
C
, then with probability greater than
1-2Kd^{-1}
,

\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{K}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}\lesssim M_{n}^{\frac{\beta}{\beta+1}-\frac{\beta^{K}}{(\beta+1)(2\beta+1)^{K-1}}}s^{\frac{\beta}{2(\beta+1)}-\frac{\beta^{K}}{2(\beta+1)(2\beta+1)^{K-1}}}\left(\frac{Ks\log d}{N}\right)^{\frac{\beta}{\beta+1}-\frac{\beta^{K+1}}{(\beta+1)(2\beta+1)^{K}}},

\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{K}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{1}\lesssim M_{n}^{\frac{\beta}{\beta+1}-\frac{\beta^{K}}{(\beta+1)(2\beta+1)^{K-1}}}s^{\frac{2\beta+1}{2(\beta+1)}-\frac{\beta^{K}}{2(\beta+1)(2\beta+1)^{K-1}}}\left(\frac{Ks\log d}{N}\right)^{\frac{\beta}{\beta+1}-\frac{\beta^{K+1}}{(\beta+1)(2\beta+1)^{K}}}.

A.6.2
Supplementary Results for Path-following Algorithm

The analysis of the path-following algorithm follows the same line as
Feng et al. (2022)
and the references therein. We only provide a sketch of the proof and refer the details to the original paper. In fact, the key difference between our proof and
Feng et al. (2022)
is established in Proposition
A.2
and Proposition
A.1
.

Lemma A.7
.

Assume the conditions of Proposition
A.2
and Assumption
3.5
hold. For
\lambda\geq\lambda_{k,tgt}
, if
\bm{\theta}\in\Omega,\left\|\bm{\theta}_{S^{*c}}\right\|_{0}\leq\widetilde{s},\omega_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta})\leq\frac{1}{2}\lambda
, and
\left\|\nabla R_{\delta_{k}}^{D_{k}}\left(\bm{\theta}^{*}\right)-\nabla R_{\delta_{k},\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1}}(\bm{\theta}^{*})\right\|_{\infty}\leq\lambda/8
, we have

\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{\bar{C}_{1}}{\rho_{-}}\left(c_{n,k}\delta_{k}^{\beta}\vee\sqrt{s}\lambda\right),

\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{1}\leq\frac{\bar{C}_{2}}{\rho_{-}}\left(\frac{c_{n,k}^{2}\delta_{k}^{2\beta}}{\lambda}\vee\sqrt{s}c_{n,k}\delta_{k}^{\beta}\vee s\lambda\right),

f_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta})-f_{\lambda}\left(\bm{\theta}^{*}\right)\leq\frac{\bar{C}_{2}}{2\rho_{-}}\left(c_{n,k}^{2}\delta_{k}^{2\beta}\vee\sqrt{s}c_{n,k}\delta_{k}^{\beta}\lambda\vee s\lambda^{2}\right),

where
f_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta})
denotes the objective function
R_{\delta_{k}}^{D_{k}}(\bm{\theta})+\lambda\|\bm{\theta}\|_{1}
and
\bar{C}_{1},\bar{C}_{2}>0
are constants that depend on
C_{2}
in Proposition
A.2
.

Proof.

Combining (
3.6
) in Assumption
3.5
with the definition of
w_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta})
, we can derive

\frac{3}{2}\lambda\left\|\left(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right)_{S^{*}}\right\|_{1}-\left(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right)^{T}\nabla R_{\delta_{k}}^{D_{k}}\left(\bm{\theta}^{*}\right)\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda\left\|\left(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right)_{S^{*c}}\right\|_{1}+\rho_{-}\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

(A.71)

By Proposition
A.2
, Proposition
A.1
, and notice the sparsity of
\bm{\theta},\bm{\theta}^{*}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right)^{T}\nabla R_{\delta_{k}}^{D_{k}}\left(\bm{\theta}^{*}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\mid\left(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right)^{T}(\underbrace{\nabla R^{D_{k}}_{\delta_{k}}(\bm{\theta}^{*})-\nabla R_{\delta_{k},\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1}}(\bm{\theta}^{*})}_{E_{1}}+\underbrace{\nabla R_{\delta_{k},\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1}}(\bm{\theta}^{*})-\nabla R(\bm{\theta}^{*})}_{E_{2}})\mid)

(A.72)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{1}\left\|E_{1}\right\|_{\infty}+C_{2}c_{n,k}\delta_{k}^{\beta}\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{2},

where
C_{2}
is the constant defined in Proposition
A.2
. Combining (
A.72
) with (
A.71
) we have

\displaystyle\rho_{-}\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}c_{n,k}\delta_{k}^{\beta}\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{2}+\left(\frac{3}{2}\lambda+\left\|E_{1}\right\|_{\infty}\right)\left\|\left(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right)_{S^{*}}\right\|_{1}

(A.73)

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{1}{2}\lambda-\left\|E_{1}\right\|_{\infty}\right)\left\|\left(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right)_{S^{*c}}\right\|_{1}.

Now we discuss two cases. If
\rho_{-}\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq 3C_{2}c_{n,k}\delta_{k}^{\beta}\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{2}
, then

\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{3}{\rho_{-}}C_{2}c_{n,k}\delta_{k}^{\beta}.

(A.74)

If
\rho_{-}\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}>3C_{2}c_{n,k}\delta_{k}^{\beta}\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{2}
, then we have

\displaystyle\frac{2\rho_{-}}{3}\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{3}{2}\lambda+\left\|E_{1}\right\|_{\infty}\right)\left\|\left(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right)_{S^{*}}\right\|_{1}

(A.75)

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{1}{2}\lambda-\left\|E_{1}\right\|_{\infty}\right)\left\|\left(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right)_{S^{*c}}\right\|_{1}.

Note that the condition of
\lambda
ensures that
\frac{1}{2}\lambda-\left\|E_{1}\right\|_{\infty}\geq 0
, hence we have

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3\sqrt{s}\left(\frac{3}{2}\lambda+\left\|E_{1}\right\|_{\infty}\right)}{2\rho_{-}}\leq\frac{3}{\rho_{-}}\sqrt{s}\lambda.

(A.76)

Combining the above two cases, we conclude that

\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{3}{\rho_{-}}\left(C_{2}c_{n,k}\delta_{k}^{\beta}\vee\sqrt{s}\lambda\right).

(A.77)

For
\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{1}
, define
\gamma=\frac{\frac{3}{2}\lambda+\left\|E_{1}\right\|_{\infty}}{\frac{1}{2}\lambda-\left\|E_{1}\right\|_{\infty}}\leq\frac{13}{3}
, and we consider two cases below.

If
\left\|\left(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right)_{S^{*c}}\right\|_{1}>2\gamma\left\|\left(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right)_{S^{*}}\right\|_{1}
, by (
A.77
) we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{s}(1+2\gamma)\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{2}

(A.78)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{29}{3}\sqrt{s}\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{29}{\rho_{-}}\left(C_{2}\sqrt{s}c_{n,k}\delta_{k}^{\beta}\vee s\lambda\right).

If
\left\|\left(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right)_{S^{*c}}\right\|_{1}>2\gamma\left\|\left(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right)_{S^{*}}\right\|_{1}
, we have

\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{1}\leq\left(1+\frac{1}{2\gamma}\right)\left\|\left(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right)_{S^{*c}}\right\|_{1}.

(A.79)

By (
A.73
) we have

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{2}\lambda-\left\|E_{1}\right\|_{\infty}\right)\left\|\left(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right)_{S^{*c}}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}c_{n,k}\delta_{k}^{\beta}\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{2}-\rho_{-}\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left(\frac{3}{2}\lambda+\left\|E_{1}\right\|_{\infty}\right)\left\|\left(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right)_{S^{*}}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}c_{n,k}\delta_{k}^{\beta}\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{2}-\rho_{-}\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left(\frac{3}{2}\lambda+\left\|E_{1}\right\|_{\infty}\right)\frac{1}{2\gamma}\left\|\left(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right)_{S^{*}}\right\|_{1}.

By the definition of
\gamma
we have

\left(\frac{1}{2}\lambda-\left\|E_{1}\right\|_{\infty}\right)\left\|\left(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right)_{S^{*c}}\right\|_{1}\leq 2\left(C_{2}c_{n,k}\delta_{k}^{\beta}\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{2}-\rho_{-}\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right).

Combine this with (
A.79
) and note that
\|E_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq\lambda/8
we have

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left(2+\frac{1}{\gamma}\right)}{\frac{1}{2}\lambda-\left\|E_{1}\right\|_{\infty}}C_{2}c_{n,k}\delta_{k}^{\beta}\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{2}

(A.80)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{56C_{2}c_{n,k}\delta_{k}^{\beta}}{3\rho_{-}}\left(\frac{C_{2}c_{n,k}\delta_{k}^{\beta}}{\lambda}\vee\sqrt{s}\right).

Conclude the two cases we have

\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{1}\leq\frac{1}{\rho_{-}}\left(29\sqrt{s}C_{2}c_{n,k}\delta_{k}^{\beta}\vee 29s\lambda\vee\frac{56C_{2}^{2}c_{n,k}\delta_{k}^{2\beta}}{3\lambda}\right).

(A.81)

Finally, note that
\bm{\xi}\in\partial\|\bm{\theta}\|_{1}
implies
\bm{\xi}^{T}(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*})\geq\|\bm{\theta}\|_{1}-\|\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{1}
, and
Assumption
3.5
gives

\nabla R_{\delta_{k}}^{D_{k}}(\bm{\theta})^{T}\left(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right)\geq R_{\delta_{k}}^{D_{k}}\left(\bm{\theta}\right)-R_{\delta_{k}}^{D_{k}}(\bm{\theta}^{*})
, therefore

\displaystyle f_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta})-f_{\lambda}\left(\bm{\theta}^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=R_{\delta_{k}}^{D_{k}}(\bm{\theta})-R_{\delta_{k}}^{D_{k}}(\bm{\theta}^{*})+\lambda\|\bm{\theta}\|_{1}-\lambda\|\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right)^{T}\left(\nabla R_{\delta_{k}}^{D_{k}}(\bm{\theta})+\lambda\bm{\xi}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\lambda\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2\rho_{-}}\left(29\sqrt{s}C_{2}c_{n,k}\delta_{k}^{\beta}\lambda\vee 29s\lambda^{2}\vee\frac{56C_{2}^{2}c_{n,k}\delta_{k}^{2\beta}}{3}\right).

Rewriting the constants we finish the proof.
∎

The next two lemmas characterize the properties of the iterates
\bm{\theta}_{k,t}^{1},\cdots
at stage
t
.

Lemma A.8

(Lemma 2 in
Feng et al. (2022)
)
.

Assume the conditions of Proposition
A.2
and Assumption
3.5
hold. For
\lambda\geq\lambda_{k,tgt}
, if
\bm{\theta}\in\Omega,\left\|\bm{\theta}_{S^{*c}}\right\|_{0}\leq\widetilde{s}
, and
\left\|\nabla R_{\delta_{k}}^{D_{k}}\left(\bm{\theta}^{*}\right)-\nabla R_{\delta_{k},\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1}}(\bm{\theta}^{*})\right\|_{\infty}\leq\lambda/8
, and
f_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta})-f_{\lambda}\left(\bm{\theta}^{*}\right)\leq\frac{\bar{C}_{2}}{2\rho_{-}}\left(c_{n,k}^{2}\delta_{k}^{2\beta}\vee\sqrt{s}c_{n,k}\delta_{k}^{\beta}\lambda\vee s\lambda^{2}\right)
,
then we have

\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{\bar{C}_{1}^{\prime}}{\rho_{-}}\left(c_{n,k}\delta_{k}^{\beta}\vee s^{1/4}\sqrt{c_{n,k}\delta_{k}\lambda}\vee\sqrt{s}\lambda\right),

\left\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{1}\leq\frac{2\bar{C}_{2}}{\rho_{-}}\left(\frac{c_{n,k}^{2}\delta_{k}^{2\beta}}{\lambda}\vee\sqrt{s}c_{n,k}\delta_{k}^{\beta}\vee s\lambda\right),

where
\bar{C}_{1}^{\prime}
depends on
\bar{C}_{1},\bar{C}_{2}
, which are constants defined in Lemma
A.7
.

Lemma A.9

(Lemma 3 in
Feng et al. (2022)
)
.

Under the same conditions of Lemma
A.8
, if we choose
\lambda_{k,tgt}=C\sqrt{\frac{c_{n,k}K\log d}{n\delta_{k}}}
for some large enough constant
C
, and
\delta_{k}=c\left(\frac{sK\log d}{nc_{n,k}}\right)^{1/(2\beta+1)}
for some constant
c>0
, then

\left\|\mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{k,t}\eta}\left(\bm{\theta},\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)_{S^{*c}}\right\|_{0}\leq\widetilde{s},

(A.82)

where
\widetilde{s}=8\left(\frac{\bar{C}_{2}}{\eta\rho_{-}}+\frac{2\bar{C}_{1}^{\prime 2}\rho_{+}^{2}}{\rho_{-}^{2}}+2C_{2}^{2}\right)\cdot s
, and
\bar{C_{1}}^{\prime},\bar{C}_{2},C_{2}
are constants defined in Lemma
A.8
, Lemma
A.7
and Proposition
A.2
, respectively.

Lemma
A.8
and Lemma
A.9
together imply that if the initialization at stage
t
is sparse and satisfies
\omega_{\lambda_{k,t}}\left(\bm{\theta}_{k,t}^{0}\right)\leq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{k,t}
, then the subsequent iterate should also retain sparsity and exhibit favorable properties. Furthermore, under Assumption
3.5
, Lemma
A.10
ensures that
f_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta}_{k,t}^{0}),f_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta}_{k,t}^{1}),\cdots
are decreasing.
Consequently, the conditions delineated in Lemma
A.8
and Lemma
A.9
persist throughout the entire path
\bm{\theta}_{k,t}^{1},\cdots
, guaranteeing both sparsity and convergence towards a local solution. This proposition is formally articulated as follows.

Proposition A.3

(Proposition 3 in
Feng et al. (2022)
)
.

Under Assumptions
3.1
-
3.5
, suppose
\delta_{k}=c\left(\frac{sK\log d}{nc_{n,k}}\right)^{1/(2\beta+1)}
for some constant
c>0
. If
\left\|\nabla R_{\delta_{k}}^{D_{k}}\left(\bm{\theta}^{*}\right)-\nabla R_{\delta_{k},\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1}}(\bm{\theta}^{*})\right\|_{\infty}\leq\lambda_{k,tgt}/8\leq\lambda_{k,t}/8
and at stage
t
, the proximal gradient method is initialized with
\bm{\theta}_{k,t}^{0}\in\Omega
satisfying

\left\|\left(\bm{\theta}_{k,t}^{0}\right)_{S^{*c}}\right\|_{0}\leq\widetilde{s}\text{ and }\omega_{\lambda_{k,t}}\left(\bm{\theta}_{k,t}^{0}\right)\leq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{k,t},

(A.83)

then for
j=1,2,\cdots
, we have

•

\left\|\left(\bm{\theta}_{k,t}^{j}\right)_{S^{*c}}\right\|_{0}\leq\widetilde{s}

•

The sequence
\left\{\bm{\theta}_{k,t}^{j}\right\}_{j=0}^{\infty}
converges towards a unique local solution
\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k,t}
satisfying the first-order optimality
\omega_{\lambda_{k,t}}\left(\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k,t}\right)\leq 0
with

\left\|\left(\bm{\theta}_{k,t}^{j}\right)_{S^{*c}}\right\|_{0}\leq\widetilde{s}

•

f_{\lambda_{k,t}}\left(\bm{\theta}_{k,t}^{j}\right)-f_{\lambda_{k,t}}\left(\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k,t}\right)\leq\left(1-\frac{\eta\rho_{-}}{4}\right)^{j}\left(f_{\lambda_{k,t}}\left(\bm{\theta}_{k,t}^{0}\right)-f_{\lambda_{k,t}}\left(\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k,t}\right)\right)
.

Lemma A.10
.

(
Nesterov (2013)
, Theorem 1)
Under the same conditions of Lemma
A.9
, we have

f_{\lambda}\left(\mathcal{S}_{\lambda\eta}(\bm{\theta},\Omega)\right)\leq f_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta})-\frac{1}{2\eta}\left\|\mathcal{S}_{\lambda\eta}(\bm{\theta},\Omega)-\bm{\theta}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

Lemma A.11
.

(
Nesterov (2013)
, Corollary 1)
Under the same conditions of Lemma
A.9
, we have

\omega_{\lambda_{t}}\left(\mathcal{S}_{\lambda\eta}(\bm{\theta},\Omega)\right)\leq\left(\frac{1}{\eta}+\rho_{+}\right)\left\|\mathcal{S}_{\lambda\eta}(\bm{\theta},\Omega)-\bm{\theta}\right\|_{2}.

Lemma A.12

(Lemma 10 in
Feng et al. (2022)
)
.

Suppose Assumption
3.5
holds. If
\lambda\geq\lambda_{k,tgt}
,
\|\nabla R^{D_{k}}_{\delta_{k}}(\bm{\theta}^{*})-\nabla R_{\delta_{k},\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1}}(\bm{\theta}^{*})\|_{\infty}\leq\lambda_{k,tgt}/8
,
\omega_{\lambda}\left(\bm{\theta}\right)\leq\frac{1}{2}\lambda
,
\left\|\bm{\theta}_{S^{*}}\right\|_{0}\leq\widetilde{s}
and
\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{\lambda}\in\Omega
is a minimizer of
f_{\lambda}
satisfying
\|(\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{\lambda})_{S^{*c}}\|_{0}\leq\widetilde{s}
, then we have

f_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta})-f_{\lambda}\left(\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{\lambda}\right)\leq\frac{\bar{C}_{2}}{\rho_{-}}\left(\delta^{2\beta}\vee\sqrt{s}\delta^{\beta}\lambda\vee s\lambda^{2}\right).

Theorem 9
.

Assume the conditions of Proposition
A.2
and Assumption
3.5
hold. By choosing
\nu=0.25,\phi=0.9,\eta\leq\frac{1}{\rho_{+}}
,
\lambda_{k,tgt}=8C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{c_{n,k}K\log d}{n\delta_{k}}}
, where
C_{1}
is defined in Proposition
A.1
and
\delta_{k}=c\left(\frac{sK\log d}{nc_{n,k}}\right)^{1/(2\beta+1)}
for some constant
c>0
, with probability greater than
1-2d^{-1}
, the final approximate local solution
\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k,tgt}
from the path-following algorithm satisfies

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k,tgt}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\left(\frac{Ks\log d}{nc_{n,k}}\right)^{\frac{\beta\vee 1}{2\beta+1}},

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k,tgt}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{1}\lesssim\sqrt{s}\left(\frac{Ks\log d}{nc_{n,k}}\right)^{\frac{\beta\vee 1}{2\beta+1}}.

Proof.

By Proposition
A.1
we have

\|\nabla R^{D_{k}}_{\delta_{k}}(\bm{\theta}^{*})-\nabla R_{\delta_{k},\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1}}(\bm{\theta}^{*})\|_{\infty}\leq C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{c_{n,k}K\log d}{n\delta_{k}}},

hence with probability greater than
1-2d^{-1}
,
\|\nabla R^{D_{k}}_{\delta_{k}}(\bm{\theta}^{*})-\nabla R_{\delta_{k},\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1}}(\bm{\theta}^{*})\|_{\infty}\leq\lambda_{k,tgt}/8
holds. We prove this theorem by induction. Note that the initialization in Algorithm
4
guarantees that

\left\|\left(\bm{\theta}_{k,0}^{0}\right)_{S^{*c}}\right\|_{0}\leq\widetilde{s}\text{ and }\omega_{\lambda_{k,0}}\left(\bm{\theta}_{k,0}^{0}\right)\leq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{k,0},

where
\widetilde{s}
is defined in Proposition
A.3
. Suppose at stage
t=1,\cdots,T-1
, we have

\left\|\left(\bm{\theta}_{k,t}^{0}\right)_{S^{*c}}\right\|_{0}\leq\widetilde{s}\text{ and }\omega_{\lambda_{k,t}}\left(\bm{\theta}_{k,t}^{0}\right)\leq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{k,t}.

By Proposition
A.3
, we know
\left\|\left(\bm{\theta}_{k,t}^{j}\right)_{S^{*c}}\right\|_{0}\leq\widetilde{s}
for
j=1,\cdots,
which implies that
\left\|\left(\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{k,t}\right)_{S^{*c}}\right\|_{0}\leq\widetilde{s}
if exists. Recall that at stage
t
, the stopping criteria requires
\omega_{\lambda_{k,t}}\left(\bm{\theta}\right)\leq\frac{1}{4}\lambda_{k,t}
, therefore it suffices to find
j
such that
\omega_{\lambda_{k,t}}\left(\bm{\theta}_{k,t}^{j}\right)\leq\frac{1}{4}\lambda_{k,t}
to finish stage
t
. By Lemma
A.11
, we have

\omega_{\lambda_{k,t}}\left(\bm{\theta}_{k,t}^{j}\right)\leq\left(\frac{1}{\eta}+\rho_{+}\right)\left\|\bm{\theta}_{k,t}^{j}-\bm{\theta}_{k,t}^{j-1}\right\|_{2}.

Recall that
\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k,t}
is defined as (
A.2
),
from Lemma
A.10
we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\eta}\left\|\bm{\theta}_{k,t}^{j}-\bm{\theta}_{k,t}^{j-1}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq f_{\lambda_{k,t}}\left(\bm{\theta}_{k,t}^{j-1}\right)-f_{\lambda_{k,t}}\left(\bm{\theta}_{t}^{j}\right)

(A.84)

\displaystyle\leq f_{\lambda_{k,t}}\left(\bm{\theta}_{k,t}^{j-1}\right)-f_{\lambda_{k,t}}\left(\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k,t}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\frac{\eta\rho_{-}}{4}\right)^{j-1}\left(f_{\lambda_{k,t}}\left(\bm{\theta}_{k,t}^{0}\right)-f_{\lambda_{k,t}}\left(\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k,t}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\frac{\eta\rho_{-}}{4}\right)^{j-1}\frac{\bar{C}_{2}}{\rho_{-}}\left(\delta_{k}^{2\beta}\vee\sqrt{s}\delta_{k}^{\beta}\lambda_{k,t}\vee s\lambda_{k,t}^{2}\right),

where the second last inequality is from Proposition
A.3
and the last inequality follows from Lemma
A.12
. Now it suffices to guarantee that

\left(\frac{1}{\eta}+\rho_{+}\right)\sqrt{2\eta\left(1-\frac{\eta\rho_{-}}{4}\right)^{j-1}\frac{\bar{C}_{2}}{\rho_{-}}\left(\delta^{2\beta}\vee\sqrt{s}\delta^{\beta}\lambda_{k,t}\vee s\lambda_{k,t}^{2}\right)}\leq\frac{1}{4}\lambda_{k,t}.

Recall that we choose
\delta_{k}=c\left(\frac{sK\log d}{nc_{n,k}}\right)^{1/(2\beta+1)}
,
\lambda_{k,t}>\lambda_{k,tgt}
and
\lambda_{k,tgt}=8C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{c_{n,k}K\log d}{n\delta_{k}}}
. With some algebra we can show that it suffices to guarantee that

j\geq\log\left(\frac{32\left(\frac{1}{\eta}+\rho_{+}\right)^{2}\eta\bar{C}_{2}s}{\rho_{-}}\right)/\log\left(\frac{4}{4-\eta\rho_{-}}\right)+1,

where the RHS is independent of
\lambda
.
∎

A.6.3
A Variation of Theorem
6

Theorem 10
.

Under Assumptions
3.1
-
3.5
, assume that the number of iterations
K\geq 2
and
\beta\geq 2
.
We set
N_{K}=N/2
,
N_{k}=\left(tN_{k+1}\right)^{1/\beta}
for
2\leq k\leq K-1
,
N_{1}=\left(\frac{tN_{2}}{(s\log d)^{\beta/(2\beta+1)}C_{N}^{\beta}}\right)^{(2\beta+1)/(2\beta^{2})}
, and

\delta_{1}=c_{1}\left(\frac{s\log d}{N_{1}}\right)^{1/(2\beta+1)},\ \lambda_{1}=c_{2}\sqrt{\frac{N_{1}K^{2}\log d}{n^{2}\delta_{1}}},

\delta_{k}=c_{1}\left(\frac{C_{N}s\log d}{N_{k}}\right)^{1/(2\beta)},\ \lambda_{k}=c_{2}\sqrt{\frac{N_{k}K^{2}\log d}{n^{2}b_{k-1}\delta_{k}}},\ b_{k-1}=c_{3}\left(\frac{C_{N}^{2\beta+1}s\log d}{N_{k}}\right)^{1/(2\beta)},2\leq k\leq K,

for some constants
c_{1},c_{2},c_{3}
and
c_{3}\geq c_{1}
.
If

N\geq t^{\frac{1}{\beta-1}}2^{K}\vee\left(\frac{t^{\frac{\beta-1/\beta^{(K-2)}}{\beta-1}}}{(s\log d)^{\beta/(2\beta+1)}C_{N}^{\beta}}\right)^{\frac{\beta^{K-2}(2\beta+1)}{2\beta^{K}-(2\beta+1)}}2^{\frac{2(K-1)\beta^{K}-(2\beta+1)}{2\beta^{K}-(2\beta+1)}},

(A.85)

where
t=s^{2\beta-1}M_{n}^{2\beta}(\log d)^{\beta-1}/C_{N}^{\beta+1}
and

N\leq C\left(n/K\right)^{2\beta/(2\beta+1)}(s\log d)^{1/(2\beta+1)}C_{N}

(A.86)

hold for some constant
C
, then
\sum_{k=1}^{K}N_{k}\leq N
and with probability greater than
1-2K/d
,

\left\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{K}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\left(\frac{C_{N}s\log d}{N}\right)^{1/2},\ \left\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{K}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{1}\lesssim\sqrt{s}\left(\frac{C_{N}s\log d}{N}\right)^{1/2}.

Proof.

First, let’s consider the case when
k=1
. For each
(X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i})\in D_{1}
, we have

\mathbb{P}(R_{i}=1)=c_{n,1},\ \mathbb{P}(R_{i}=0)=1-c_{n,1}.

and
N_{1}=nc_{n,1}/K
.
According to Theorem
1
, by selecting
\delta_{1}=c_{1}\left(\frac{s\log d}{N_{1}}\right)^{1/(2\beta+1)}
and
\lambda_{1}=c_{2}\sqrt{\frac{N_{1}K^{2}\log d}{n^{2}\delta_{1}}}
, with probability greater than
1-2d^{-1}
, we obtain

\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{1}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}\lesssim\left(\frac{s\log d}{N_{1}}\right)^{\beta/(2\beta+1)},

(A.87)

\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{1}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{1}\lesssim\sqrt{s}\left(\frac{s\log d}{N_{1}}\right)^{\beta/(2\beta+1)}.

(A.88)

Next, we will establish the bound for
\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{K}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{1}
. The result for
\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{K}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}
follows similarly.
Following the same proof as in Theorem
6
, we can show that
\mathbb{P}\left((X,\bm{Z})\in S_{k}\right)\asymp b_{k-1}
.
Note that

N_{k}=n\mathbb{E}(R_{i})/K=nc_{n,k}\mathbb{P}\left((X,\bm{Z})\in S_{k}\right)/K
. Applying Theorem
1
, we select

\delta_{k}\asymp\left(\frac{Ks\log d}{nc_{n,k}}\right)^{1/(2\beta+1)}\asymp\left(\frac{s\log d\mathbb{P}\left((X,\bm{Z})\in S_{k}\right)}{N_{k}}\right)^{1/(2\beta+1)}\asymp\left(\frac{b_{k-1}s\log d}{N_{k}}\right)^{1/(2\beta+1)},

(A.89)

and
\lambda_{k}=c_{k,2}\sqrt{\frac{N_{k}K^{2}\log d}{n^{2}b_{k-1}\delta_{k}}}
,
to ensure, with probability greater than
1-2d^{-1}

\left\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{1}\lesssim\sqrt{s}\left(\frac{\mathbb{P}\left((X,\bm{Z})\in S_{k}\right)s\log d}{N_{k}}\right)^{\beta/(2\beta+1)}\lesssim\sqrt{s}\left(\frac{b_{k-1}s\log d}{N_{k}}\right)^{\beta/(2\beta+1)}.

(A.90)

It’s important to note that for Proposition
A.2
to hold which enables us to apply Theorem
1
, we need to choose
b_{k-1}
such that

b_{k-1}\geq C_{N}\delta_{k}\text{\ and\ }b_{k-1}\geq 2\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{1}M_{n}.

(A.91)

To meet the first condition and by (
A.89
), we have
\left(\frac{C_{N}^{2\beta+1}s\log d}{N_{k}}\right)^{1/(2\beta)}=O(b_{k-1})
. Therefore, we choose
b_{k-1}=c_{3}\left(\frac{C_{N}^{2\beta+1}s\log d}{N_{k}}\right)^{1/(2\beta)}
for some constant
c_{3}
. Substituting this into (
A.90
), we get

\left\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{1}\lesssim\sqrt{s}\left(\frac{C_{N}s\log d}{N_{k}}\right)^{1/2},

(A.92)

which implies
\left\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{K}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{1}\lesssim\sqrt{s}\left(\frac{C_{N}s\log d}{N}\right)^{1/2}
, and
\left\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{K}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\left(\frac{C_{N}s\log d}{N}\right)^{1/2}
follows similarly.

Now, let’s explore the assumption (
A.86
).
By definition, we have
N_{k}=\sum_{(X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i})\in D_{k}}\mathbb{E}\left(R_{i}\right)=n\mathbb{E}(R_{i})/K=nc_{n,k}\mathbb{P}\left((X,\bm{Z})\in S_{k}\right)/K
, where
0<c_{n,k}<1
is defined as

\mathbb{P}(R_{i}=1\mid Y_{i},X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i},\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1})=\mathbb{P}(R_{i}=1\mid X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i},\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1})=c_{n,k}\cdot\ind\{(X_{i},\bm{Z}_{i})\in S_{k}\}.

To ensure that
0<c_{n,k}\leq 1
, we require

N_{k}K\leq n\mathbb{P}\left((X,\bm{Z})\in S_{k}\right),\ 2\leq k\leq K,

and it suffices to ensure that
N_{k}K\leq Cb_{k-1}n
, where
b_{k-1}=c_{3}\left(\frac{C_{N}^{2\beta+1}s\log d}{N_{k}}\right)^{1/(2\beta)}
.
Subsequently, some calculation yields
N_{k}\leq C\left(n/K\right)^{2\beta/(2\beta+1)}(s\log d)^{1/(2\beta+1)}C_{N}
for some constant
C
, which is provided in (
A.86
).

Next we check that the second condition in (
A.91
), i.e.,
b_{k-1}\geq 2\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{1}M_{n}
holds for the chosen
b_{k-1}
and
N_{1},\cdots,N_{K}
. Recall that we choose
N_{K}=N/2
,
N_{k}=\left(tN_{k+1}\right)^{1/\beta}
for
2\leq k\leq K-1
,
N_{1}=\left(\frac{tN_{2}}{(s\log d)^{\beta/(2\beta+1)}C_{N}^{\beta}}\right)^{(2\beta+1)/2\beta^{2}}
, where
t=s^{2\beta-1}M_{n}^{2\beta}(\log d)^{\beta-1}/C_{N}^{\beta+1}
. When
k=2
, by (
A.88
) it suffices to show that

N_{2}=O\left(\frac{C_{N}^{2\beta+1}\log d}{s^{\beta-1}M_{n}^{2\beta}}\left(\frac{N_{1}}{s\log d}\right)^{2\beta^{2}/(2\beta+1)}\right),

(A.93)

and when
2\leq k\leq K
, by (
A.92
) we need to ensure that

N_{k}=O\left(\frac{C_{N}^{\beta+1}\log d}{s^{\beta-1}M_{n}^{2\beta}}\left(\frac{N_{k-1}}{s\log d}\right)^{\beta}\right).

(A.94)

It is easy to check that the choice of
N_{k}
above satisfies both (
A.93
) and (
A.94
).

Lastly, we demonstrate that the selected values
N_{1},\cdots,N_{K}
satisfy the condition
\sum_{k=1}^{K}N_{k}\leq N
. Our objective is to prove that for all
0\leq j\leq K-2
,

N_{K-j}\leq\frac{N}{2^{j+1}},

(A.95)

and
N_{1}\leq\frac{N}{2^{K-1}}
. These inequalities together imply that
\sum_{j=1}^{K}N_{j}\leq\frac{N}{2^{K-1}}+\sum_{j=1}^{K-1}\frac{N}{2^{j}}=N
.
Given
N_{K}=N/2
,
N_{k}=\left(tN_{k+1}\right)^{1/\beta}
for
2\leq k\leq K-1
, we can derive

N_{K-j}=t^{\sum_{i=1}^{j}1/\beta^{j}}\left(\frac{N}{2}\right)^{1/\beta^{j}}=t^{\frac{1-1/\beta^{j}}{\beta-1}}\left(\frac{N}{2}\right)^{1/\beta^{j}}.

Thus to show (
A.95
), it suffices to prove
t^{\frac{1-1/\beta^{j}}{\beta-1}}\left(\frac{N}{2}\right)^{1/\beta^{j}}\leq\frac{N}{2^{j+1}}
for all
0\leq j\leq K-2
. Some calculations yield

N\geq t^{\frac{1}{\beta-1}}2^{\frac{(j+1)\beta^{j}-1}{\beta^{j}-1}}.

Noting that
\frac{(j+1)\beta^{j}-1}{\beta^{j}-1}=j+1+\frac{j}{\beta^{j}-1}\leq K
, we have
N\geq t^{\frac{1}{\beta-1}}2^{K}
in (
A.85
), ensuring the result.
To show that
N_{1}\leq\frac{N}{2^{K-1}}
, first note that
N_{1}=\left(\frac{tN_{2}}{(s\log d)^{\beta/(2\beta+1)}C_{N}^{\beta}}\right)^{(2\beta+1)/2\beta^{2}}
and

N_{2}=t^{\frac{1-1/\beta^{(K-2)}}{\beta-1}}\left(\frac{N}{2}\right)^{1/\beta^{(K-2)}}
. We need to verify that

\left(\frac{t^{\frac{\beta-1/\beta^{(K-2)}}{\beta-1}}\left(\frac{N}{2}\right)^{1/\beta^{(K-2)}}}{(s\log d)^{\beta/(2\beta+1)}C_{N}^{\beta}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+1}{2\beta^{2}}}=\left(\frac{t^{\frac{\beta-1/\beta^{(K-2)}}{\beta-1}}}{(s\log d)^{\beta/(2\beta+1)}C_{N}^{\beta}}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+1}{2\beta^{2}}}\left(\frac{N}{2}\right)^{\frac{2\beta+1}{2\beta^{K}}}\leq\frac{N}{2^{K-1}},

which implies
N\geq 2^{\frac{2(K-1)\beta^{K}-(2\beta+1)}{2\beta^{K}-(2\beta+1)}}\left(\frac{t^{\frac{\beta-1/\beta^{(K-2)}}{\beta-1}}}{(s\log d)^{\beta/(2\beta+1)}C_{N}^{\beta}}\right)^{\frac{\beta^{K-2}(2\beta+1)}{2\beta^{K}-(2\beta+1)}}
, and this is provided by the second part of (
A.85
).

∎

A.7
Justification of Assumption
3.5

In this section, we verify that the restricted strong convexity and restricted smoothness condition hold w.h.p for the class of conditional mean model. For simplicity, we
assume
Y\sim\operatorname{Uniform}(\{-1,1\})
and
\gamma(y)=1/\mathbb{P}(Y=y)=2
is known. The model is defined as

X=\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{Z}+\mu Y+u,

where we assume
\bm{Z}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
is a zero-mean sub-Gaussian vector with parameter
\sigma^{2}
,
\bm{Z}\perp u
, and
\mu>0
. We first verify the RSC/RSM condition for the case when
0<\beta\leq 1
, and the results for the case
\beta>1
are similar.

For
k=1
when data are uniformly sampled from
D_{1}
, the proof in
Feng et al. (2022)
can be easily adapted to verify the RSC/RSM conditions for
0<\beta\leq 1
. In the following, we focus on the proof of Assumption
3.5
on the set
\Omega=\{\bm{\theta}:\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}\lesssim\delta_{k}\}
at the
k
th iteration for any
2\leq k\leq K
. We apply a proper kernel function
K
satisfying (i)
K
has bounded support on
[-1,1]
, (ii)
\|K\|_{\infty},\left\|K^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty},\left\|K^{\prime\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\text{ and }\widetilde{K}_{\beta}=-\int K^{\prime}(t)t^{\beta}dt>0
are bounded above by universal constants, and (iii)
K^{\prime}(t)>0
for
t<0
and
K(\cdot)
is symmetric. To verify that Assumption
3.5
holds w.h.p, it suffices to show that the following sparse eigenvalue condition holds w.h.p:

\displaystyle\rho_{\max}=\sup\left\{\bm{v}^{T}\nabla^{2}R_{\delta_{k}}^{D_{k}}(\bm{\theta})\bm{v}:\|\bm{v}\|_{2}=1,\|\bm{v}\|_{0}\leq Cs,\bm{\theta}\in\Omega,\|\bm{\theta}\|_{0}\leq Cs\right\}<C_{1}\delta_{k}^{\beta-1}c_{n,k},

(A.96)

\displaystyle\rho_{\min}=\inf\left\{\bm{v}^{T}\nabla^{2}R_{\delta_{k}}^{D_{k}}(\bm{\theta})\bm{v}:\|\bm{v}\|_{2}=1,\|\bm{v}\|_{0}\leq Cs,\bm{\theta}\in\Omega,\|\bm{\theta}\|_{0}\leq Cs\right\}>C_{2}\delta_{k}^{\beta-1}c_{n,k},

(A.97)

for some constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
, where
0<\beta\leq 1
. We let
\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{Z}}=\mathbf{Cov}(\bm{Z})
and
g(\cdot)
be the p.d.f of the error
u
.
Denote
\nabla^{2}R_{\delta_{k}}(\bm{\theta})=\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla^{2}R_{\delta_{k}}^{D_{k}}(\bm{\theta})|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1}\right]
as the population Hessian.

Proposition A.4
.

Under the setup above, suppose
g(\cdot)
is an even function and satisfies the Hölder condition
|g(y)-g(x)|\leq L_{2}|x-y|^{\beta}
for any
-\frac{3\mu}{2}\leq x\leq y\leq-\frac{\mu}{2}
, and
\mathop{\mathrm{sign}}(t)(g(-\mu+t)-g(-\mu))\geq L_{1}|t|^{\beta}
. If we assume
\log d\gtrsim\log n
and

\sqrt{\frac{c_{n,k}s\log\left(\frac{(ds\log{d)^{2}}}{c_{n,k}\delta_{k}}\right)}{n_{k}\delta_{k}^{3}}}+\frac{s\log(d\vee n_{k})}{n_{k}\delta_{k}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{(ds\log{d)^{2}}}{c_{n,k}\delta_{k}}\right)\lesssim\delta_{k}^{\beta-1}c_{n,k},

(A.98)

then with probability greater than
1-c(\log n)^{-1}
, it holds that

\rho_{\min}\geq L_{1}\widetilde{K}_{\beta}\delta_{k}^{\beta-1}c_{n,k}\lambda_{\min}\left(\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{Z}}\right)/2,~~\ \rho_{\max}\leq 2L_{2}\widetilde{K}_{\beta}\delta_{k}^{\beta-1}c_{n,k}\lambda_{\max}\left(\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{Z}}\right).

Proof.

Write

\bm{v}^{T}\nabla^{2}R^{D_{k}}_{\delta_{k}}(\bm{\theta})\bm{v}=\bm{v}^{T}\nabla^{2}R_{\delta_{k}}(\bm{\theta}^{*})\bm{v}+\bm{v}^{T}(\nabla^{2}R^{D_{k}}_{\delta_{k}}(\bm{\theta})-\nabla^{2}R_{\delta_{k}}(\bm{\theta}))\bm{v}+\bm{v}^{T}(\nabla^{2}R_{\delta_{k}}(\bm{\theta})-\nabla^{2}R_{\delta_{k}}(\bm{\theta}^{*}))\bm{v}.

(A.99)

By Lemma
A.13
we have

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{T}\nabla^{2}R_{\delta_{k}}(\bm{\theta^{*}})\bm{v}\leq L_{2}\widetilde{K}_{\beta}\delta_{k}^{\beta-1}c_{n,k}\lambda_{\max}\left(\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{Z}}\right),\ \bm{v}^{T}\nabla^{2}R_{\delta_{k}}(\bm{\theta^{*}})\bm{v}\geq L_{1}\widetilde{K}_{\beta}\delta_{k}^{\beta-1}c_{n,k}\lambda_{\min}\left(\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{Z}}\right).

(A.100)

By Lemma
A.14
we have with probability greater than
1-c(\log n)^{-1}
,

\sup_{||\bm{v}||_{2}=1,||\bm{v}||_{0}\leq Cs}\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\Omega,||\bm{\theta}||_{0}\leq Cs}\bigl|\bm{v}^{T}(\nabla^{2}R_{\delta_{k}}^{n}(\bm{\theta})-\nabla^{2}R_{\delta_{k}}(\bm{\theta})\bm{v}\bigr|\lesssim\delta_{k}^{\beta-1}c_{n,k}.

Then it remains to show that
|\bm{v}^{T}(\nabla^{2}R_{\delta_{k}}(\bm{\theta})-\nabla^{2}R_{\delta_{k}}(\bm{\theta}^{*}))\bm{v}|\lesssim\delta_{k}^{\beta-1}c_{n,k}
on the set
\Omega=\{\bm{\theta}:\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}\lesssim\delta_{k}\}
. Given
\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1}
, the population Hessian difference is

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{T}(\nabla^{2}R_{\delta_{k}}(\bm{\theta})-\nabla^{2}R_{\delta_{k}}(\bm{\theta}^{*}))\bm{v}

(A.101)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\gamma(Y)\frac{(\bm{v}^{T}\bm{Z})^{2}}{\delta_{k}^{2}}\left(K^{\prime}\left(\frac{Y(X-\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{Z})}{\delta_{k}}\right)-K^{\prime}\left(\frac{Y(X-\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{Z})}{\delta_{k}}\right)\right)R\mid\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(\bm{v}^{T}\bm{Z})^{2}}{\delta_{k}^{2}}\left(K^{\prime}\left(\frac{X-\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{Z}}{\delta_{k}}\right)-K^{\prime}\left(\frac{X-\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{Z}}{\delta_{k}}\right)\right)R\mid\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1},Y=1\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(\bm{v}^{T}\bm{Z})^{2}}{\delta_{k}^{2}}\left(K^{\prime}\left(\frac{-X+\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{Z}}{\delta_{k}}\right)-K^{\prime}\left(\frac{-X+\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{Z}}{\delta_{k}}\right)\right)R\mid\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1},Y=-1\right].

We bound the first term and similar for the second term. Denote
\frac{-b_{k-1}\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{k-1}\|_{2}+(\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1}-\bm{\theta}^{*})^{T}\bm{z}}{\delta_{k}}:=S_{u}^{*-}
and
\frac{b_{k-1}\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{k-1}\|_{2}+(\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1}-\bm{\theta}^{*})^{T}\bm{z}}{\delta_{k}}:=S_{u}^{*+}
.
Recall that in Proposition
A.2
, we showed that
S_{u}^{*+}\geq\frac{b_{k-1}\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{k-1}\|_{2}-\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{1}M_{n}}{\delta_{k}}\geq C_{N}/2
and
S_{u}^{*-}\leq-C_{N}/2.
for some large constant
C_{N}
. Then
\mathrm{supp}\{K\}=[-1,1]\subseteq[S_{u}^{*-},S_{u}^{*+}]
. And in the conditional mean model, we have
f(x\mid\bm{z},Y=y)=g(x-\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{z}-\mu y)
.

\displaystyle\text{First term}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(\bm{v}^{T}\bm{Z})^{2}}{\delta_{k}^{2}}\left(K^{\prime}\left(\frac{X-\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{Z}}{\delta_{k}}\right)-K^{\prime}\left(\frac{X-\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{Z}}{\delta_{k}}\right)\right)R\mid\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1},Y=1,X,\bm{Z}\right]\mid\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1},Y=1\right]

(A.102)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(\bm{v}^{T}\bm{Z})^{2}}{\delta_{k}^{2}}\left(K^{\prime}\left(\frac{X-\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{Z}}{\delta_{k}}\right)-K^{\prime}\left(\frac{X-\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{Z}}{\delta_{k}}\right)\right)c_{n,k}\ind\{(X,\bm{Z})\in S_{k}\}\mid\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1},Y=1\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{c_{n,k}}{\delta_{k}^{2}}\int(\bm{v}^{T}\bm{Z})^{2}\int_{-b_{k-1}||w_{k-1}||_{2}+\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1}^{T}\bm{z}}^{b_{k-1}||w_{k-1}||_{2}+\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1}^{T}\bm{z}}\left[K^{\prime}\left(\frac{X-\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{Z}}{\delta_{k}}\right)-K^{\prime}\left(\frac{X-\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{Z}}{\delta_{k}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle g(x-\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{z}-\mu)\,dxf(\bm{Z})\,dz

\displaystyle=\frac{c_{n,k}}{\delta_{k}^{2}}\int(\bm{v}^{T}\bm{Z})^{2}\int_{S_{u}^{*-}}^{S_{u}^{*+}}\left[K^{\prime}\left(\frac{(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*})^{T}\bm{Z}}{\delta_{k}}+u\right)-K^{\prime}\left(u\right)\right]g(\delta_{k}u-\mu)\,duf(\bm{Z})\,dz

\displaystyle=\frac{c_{n,k}}{\delta_{k}^{2}}\int(\bm{v}^{T}\bm{Z})^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[K^{\prime}\left(\frac{(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*})^{T}\bm{Z}}{\delta_{k}}+u\right)-K^{\prime}\left(u\right)\right](g(\delta_{k}u-\mu)-g(-\mu))\,duf(\bm{Z})\,dz.

Note that

\left|K^{\prime}\left(\frac{(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*})^{T}\bm{Z}}{\delta_{k}}+u\right)-K^{\prime}\left(u\right)\right|=\left|\int_{u}^{u+\frac{(\bm{\theta}^{*}-\bm{\theta})^{T}\bm{Z}}{\delta_{k}}}K^{\prime\prime}(t)\,dt\right|\leq||K^{\prime\prime}||_{\infty}\frac{(\bm{\theta}^{*}-\bm{\theta})^{T}\bm{Z}}{\delta_{k}}

and

|g(\delta_{k}u-\mu)-g(-\mu)|\leq L_{2}|\delta_{k}u|^{\beta}\leq C|\delta_{k}|^{\beta}.

We have

\displaystyle(\ref{eq: hessian diff})

\displaystyle\lesssim c_{n,k}\delta_{k}^{\beta-2}\int(\bm{v}^{T}\bm{Z})^{2}(\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*})^{T}\bm{Z}f(\bm{Z})\,d\bm{Z}

(A.103)

\displaystyle\leq c_{n,k}\delta_{k}^{\beta-2}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[(\bm{v}^{T}\bm{Z})^{4}]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[((\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*})^{T}\bm{Z})^{2}]}

\displaystyle\leq c_{n,k}\delta_{k}^{\beta-2}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[(\bm{v}^{T}\bm{Z})^{4}]}\sqrt{M_{1}}||\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}||_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim c_{n,k}\delta_{k}^{\beta-2}\delta_{k}=c_{n,k}\delta_{k}^{\beta-1}.

The last inequality is because
\bm{Z}
is a sub-Gaussian vector with a bounded sub-Gaussian norm and thus
\bm{v}^{T}\bm{Z}
is also a sub-Gaussian random variable with bounded moment. Combining the bound for the three terms in (
A.99
), we finish the proof.
∎

Remark 5
.

If we plug in
c_{n,k}=\frac{NkK}{n\mathbb{P}((X,Z)\in S_{k})}\asymp\frac{N_{k}K}{nb_{k-1}}
and
\delta_{k}\asymp(\frac{s\log d}{N_{k}}b_{k-1})^{\frac{1}{2\beta+1}}
, condition (
A.98
) is equivalent to
\frac{b_{k-1}s(\log d)^{3+\beta^{-1}}}{N_{k}}=O(1)
.

Lemma A.13
.

Suppose
\widetilde{K}_{\beta}=-\int K^{\prime}(t)t^{\beta}dt>0
,
g(\cdot)
is an even function and satisfies the Hölder condition
|g(y)-g(x)|\leq L_{2}|x-y|^{\beta}
for any
-\frac{3\mu}{2}\leq x\leq y\leq-\frac{\mu}{2}
, and
\mathop{\mathrm{sign}}(t)(g(-\mu+t)-g(-\mu))\geq L_{1}|t|^{\beta}
, then for all unit vector
\bm{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we have

\bm{v}^{T}\nabla^{2}R_{\delta_{k}}(\bm{\theta^{*}})\bm{v}\leq L_{2}\widetilde{K}_{\beta}\delta_{k}^{\beta-1}c_{n,k}\lambda_{\max}\left(\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{Z}}\right),\ \bm{v}^{T}\nabla^{2}R_{\delta_{k}}(\bm{\theta^{*}})\bm{v}\geq L_{1}\widetilde{K}_{\beta}\delta_{k}^{\beta-1}c_{n,k}\lambda_{\min}\left(\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{Z}}\right),

where
\|\bm{v}\|_{2}=1
and
\|\bm{v}\|_{0}\leq Cs
.

Proof.

Denote
\frac{-b_{k-1}\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{k-1}\|_{2}+(\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1}-\bm{\theta}^{*})^{T}\bm{z}}{\delta_{k}}:=S_{u}^{*-}
and
\frac{b_{k-1}\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{k-1}\|_{2}+(\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1}-\bm{\theta}^{*})^{T}\bm{z}}{\delta_{k}}:=S_{u}^{*+}
.
Recall that in Proposition
A.2
we showed that

S_{u}^{*+}\geq\frac{b_{k-1}\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{k-1}\|_{2}-\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{1}M_{n}}{\delta_{k}}\geq C_{N}/2,

and similarly,

S_{u}^{*-}\leq-C_{N}/2.

By definition

\nabla^{2}R^{D_{k}}_{\delta_{k}}(\bm{\theta})=-\frac{K}{n}\sum_{(x_{i},\bm{z}_{i})\in D_{k}}\gamma(y_{i})\frac{y_{i}^{2}\bm{z}_{i}\bm{z}_{i}^{T}}{\delta_{k}^{2}}K^{\prime}(\frac{y_{i}(x_{i}-\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{z}_{i})}{\delta_{k}})R_{i}.

Then,

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{T}\nabla^{2}R_{\delta_{k}}(\bm{\theta})\bm{v}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{c_{n,k}}{\delta_{k}^{2}}\int_{\bm{z}}\int_{-b_{k-1}\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{k-1}\|_{2}+\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1}^{T}\bm{z}}^{b_{k-1}\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{k-1}\|_{2}+\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1}^{T}\bm{z}}\left(\bm{v}^{T}\bm{z}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\cdot\left(K^{\prime}\left(\frac{x-\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{z}}{\delta_{k}}\right)g\left(x-\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{z}-\mu\right)+K^{\prime}\left(\frac{-(x-\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{z})}{\delta_{k}}\right)g\left(x-\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{z}+\mu\right)\right)f(\bm{z})dxd\bm{z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{c_{n,k}}{\delta_{k}}\int_{\bm{z}}(\bm{v}^{T}\bm{z})^{2}\int_{S_{u}^{-}}^{S_{u}^{+}}\left(K^{\prime}(t)g\left(\delta_{k}t+\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{z}-\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{z}-\mu\right)+K^{\prime}(-t)g\left(\delta_{k}t+\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{z}-\bm{\theta}^{*T}\bm{z}+\mu\right)\right)f(\bm{z})dtd\bm{z}.

We choose
K(t)
such that
K^{\prime}(t)
is an odd function, then we have

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{T}\nabla^{2}R_{\delta_{k}}(\bm{\theta^{*}})\bm{v}

(A.104)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{c_{n,k}}{\delta_{k}}\int_{\bm{z}}(\bm{v}^{T}\bm{z})^{2}\int_{S_{u}^{*-}}^{S_{u}^{*+}}K^{\prime}(t)\left(g\left(\delta_{k}t-\mu\right)-g\left(-\delta_{k}t-\mu\right)\right)dtf(\bm{z})d\bm{z}.

(A.105)

By the choice of the kernel function we can ensure that

\delta_{k}t-\mu,-\delta_{k}t-\mu\in[\frac{-3\mu}{2},\frac{-\mu}{2}].

Note that
\widetilde{K}_{\beta}=-\int K^{\prime}(t)t^{\beta}dt>0
and
g(\cdot)
satisfies the Hölder condition, then

L_{1}\widetilde{K}_{\beta}\delta_{k}^{\beta-1}c_{n,k}\lambda_{\min}\left(\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{Z}}\right)\leq\bm{v}^{T}\nabla^{2}R_{\delta_{k}}(\bm{\theta^{*}})\bm{v}\leq L_{2}\widetilde{K}_{\beta}\delta_{k}^{\beta-1}c_{n,k}\lambda_{\max}\left(\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{Z}}\right).

∎

Lemma A.14
.

If we assume
\log d\gtrsim\log n
and

\sqrt{\frac{c_{n,k}s\log\left(\frac{(dslog{d)^{2}}}{c_{n,k}\delta_{k}}\right)}{n_{k}\delta_{k}^{3}}}+\frac{s\log(d\vee n_{k})}{n_{k}\delta_{k}^{2}}\log\left(\frac{(dslog{d)^{2}}}{c_{n,k}\delta_{k}}\right)\lesssim\delta_{k}^{\beta-1}c_{n,k},

(A.106)

then with probability greater than
1-c(\log n_{k})^{-1}
, it holds that

\sup_{||\bm{v}||_{2}=1,||\bm{v}||_{0}\leq Cs}\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\Omega,||\bm{\theta}||_{0}\leq Cs}\bigl|\bm{v}^{T}(\nabla^{2}R_{\delta_{k}}^{n}(\bm{\theta})-\nabla^{2}R_{\delta_{k}}(\bm{\theta})\bm{v}\bigr|\lesssim\delta_{k}^{\beta-1}c_{n,k},

(A.107)

where
n_{k}=\frac{n}{K}
, and
c
is some positive constant.

Proof.

Consider the class of functions

\mathcal{F}=\Bigl\{f_{\bm{v},\bm{\theta}}(x,y,\bm{z},R)=-\,y\,(\bm{v}^{T}\bm{z})^{2}\,K^{\prime}\!\left(\frac{x-\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{z}}{\delta_{k}}\right)R:\bm{v},\bm{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},||\bm{v}||_{2}=1,\bm{\theta}\in\Omega,||\bm{v}||_{0},||\bm{\theta}||_{0}\leq Cs\Bigr\}.

(A.108)

Our goal reduces to controlling the empirical process

\displaystyle\|G_{n}\|_{\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\Biggl|n_{k}^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}\Bigl(f(x_{i},y_{i},\bm{z}_{i},R_{i})-\mathbb{E}\,f(X,Y,\bm{Z},R)\Bigr)\Biggr|

(A.109)

\displaystyle=n_{k}^{1/2}\delta_{k}^{2}\sup_{||\bm{v}||_{2}=1,||\bm{v}||_{0}\leq Cs}\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\Omega,||\bm{\theta}||_{0}\leq Cs}\bigl|\bm{v}^{T}(\nabla^{2}R_{\delta_{k}}^{n}(\bm{\theta})-\nabla^{2}R_{\delta_{k}}(\bm{\theta})\bm{v}\bigr|.

(A.110)

To achieve this, we will apply the maximal inequality
A.15
(due to
Chernozhukov et al. (2014)
).
In order to control the metric entropy, we introduce

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{1}(q)=\{(x,y,\bm{z},R)\mapsto(\bm{v}^{T}\bm{z})^{q}:||\bm{v}||_{2}=1,||\bm{v}||_{0}\leq Cs\},

(A.111)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{2}=\{(x,y,\bm{z},R)\mapsto K^{\prime}((x-\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{z})/\delta_{k}):\ \bm{\theta}\in\Omega,||\bm{\theta}||_{0}\leq Cs\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{3}=\{(x,y,\bm{z},R)\mapsto yR\}.

For
\mathcal{F}_{3}
, it holds trivially that

\log\sup_{Q}\mathcal{N}(\epsilon,\mathcal{F}_{3},\|\cdot\|_{Q,2})\lesssim s\log(e/\epsilon).

For
\mathcal{F}_{1}(1)
, we know it is contained in the union of at most
\binom{d}{Cs}
VC-subgraph classes of functions with VC indices bounded by
C^{\prime}s
, and thus for the envelope
F_{1}=\sup_{||\bm{v}||_{2}=1,||\bm{v}||_{0}\leq Cs}\ ||\bm{v}^{T}\bm{z}||_{2}
, we have

\log\sup_{Q}\mathcal{N}\bigl(\epsilon\|F_{1}\|_{Q,2},\ \mathcal{F}_{1}(1),\|\cdot\|_{Q,2}\bigr)\ \lesssim\ s\log d+s\log(e/\epsilon).

For
\mathcal{F}_{2}
, notice that
\mathcal{F}_{2}^{\prime}:=\bigl\{(x,y,\bm{z},R)\mapsto x-\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{z}\bigr\}
is contained in the union of at most
\binom{d}{Cs}
VC-subgraph classes of functions with VC indices bounded by
C^{\prime}s
. By assumption
K^{\prime}
is of bounded variation, i.e., it can be written as the difference of two bounded nondecreasing functions. By Lemma 2.6.18 of
Vaart and Wellner (1996)
the VC-subgraph property is preserved,
and we obtain

\log\sup_{Q}\mathcal{N}\bigl(\epsilon\|K^{\prime}\|_{\infty},\ \mathcal{F}_{2},\ \|\cdot\|_{Q,2}\bigr)\ \lesssim\ s\log d+s\log(e/\epsilon).

Since
\mathcal{F}
is contained in the class of pointwise product
\mathcal{F}_{1}(2)\cdot\mathcal{F}_{2}\cdot\mathcal{F}_{3}
,
applying Lemma A.6 of
Chernozhukov et al. (2014)
, we obtain that for the choice of envelope

F=F_{1}^{2}\,\|K^{\prime}\|_{\infty}=\sup_{||\bm{v}||_{2}=1,||\bm{v}||_{0}\leq Cs}|\bm{v}^{T}\bm{z}|^{2}\,\|K^{\prime}\|_{\infty},

it holds that

\log\sup_{Q}\mathcal{N}\bigl(\epsilon\|F\|_{Q,2},\ \mathcal{F},\ \|\cdot\|_{Q,2}\bigr)\ \lesssim\ s\log d+s\log(e/\epsilon).

Thus, we could choose
v=s,a=d^{C1}
for some
C_{1}
large enough in Lemma
A.15
. Next, we calculate bound for
\rho^{2}
,
||F||_{P,2}
and
||M||_{P,2}
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(f^{2}\mid\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[(\bm{v}^{T}\bm{z})^{4}K^{\prime 2}\left(\frac{x-\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{z}}{\delta_{k}}\right)R\mid\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1}\right]

(A.112)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left((\bm{v}^{T}\bm{z})^{4}K^{\prime 2}\left(\frac{x-\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{z}}{\delta_{k}}\right)R\mid\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1},x,\bm{z}\right)\mid\widehat{\bm{\theta}}_{k-1}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[(\bm{v}^{T}\bm{z})^{4}K^{\prime 2}\left(\frac{x-\bm{\theta}^{T}\bm{z}}{\delta_{k}}\right)c_{n,k}\ind\{(x,\bm{z})\in S_{k}\}\mid\widehat{\theta}_{k-1}\right]
